- `dc_yl`/`dc_yh` given an absolute `[0, viewheight)` clamp in `R_DrawMaskedColumn` (the existing `mfloorclip`/`mceilingclip` clipping only bounds them *relative to other geometry*, not to the screen itself)
- All 6 column-drawing functions in `r_draw.c` (`R_DrawColumn`, `R_DrawColumnLow`, `R_DrawFuzzColumn`, `R_DrawFuzzColumnLow`, `R_DrawTranslatedColumn`, `R_DrawTranslatedColumnLow`) given the same unconditional-instead-of-`#ifdef RANGECHECK` bounds check, failing safe via early `return` instead of `I_Error`
- A genuinely missing `&127` source-index mask in `R_DrawTranslatedColumn`/`R_DrawTranslatedColumnLow` (every other column function masks `dc_source[...]`'s index; these two didn't) — a real bug, fixed, but not *this* bug, since this function is for color-translated monster variants never reached in the solo repro

Each of these was verified safe via direct value-dumping (`EM_ASM` writing into a `window.__xdbg` array, read back via Playwright's `page.evaluate()` after the crash — far more reliable than `fprintf`/`console.log`, which were repeatedly found to silently lose buffered output when the WASM module aborted mid-frame) — `dc_x`, `dc_yl`, `dc_yh`, `dc_source`, `dc_colormap`, and even the fully-computed `dest`/`destEnd` framebuffer pointers were ALL confirmed sane, right up to and including the very last successful draw call before the crash.

**The actual bug**, found by adding the same diagnostic one level up, at the top of `R_DrawVisSprite` (which fires once per *sprite* rather than once per *column*): `vis->patch` — a sprite-frame lump index resolved earlier in `R_ProjectSprite` via `sprframe->lump[rot]` — was `-1` for the sprite immediately following the last successfully-drawn one. `-1` is `sprframe`'s own "no such rotation/frame" sentinel value, used internally, but **never checked before being passed to `W_CacheLumpNum(vis->patch+firstspritelump, ...)`** in `R_DrawVisSprite`. With `vis->patch=-1`, this resolves to `firstspritelump-1` — a real, but completely unrelated, WAD lump (off by one into whatever precedes the sprite lump range), reinterpreted as a `patch_t`. Its bytes happened to decode as `patchWidth=10776` — a width over 50x larger than any real sprite — which is *why* the existing `texturecolumn` bounds fix never caught it: the check passed against this nonsense width, just not against any data that was actually a valid sprite. Every downstream value derived from this (`column`, `dc_source`) was a coherent pointer into *some* real WAD lump data, which is exactly why all of those individually checked out as "sane, in-bounds" pointers right up until the moment a far-enough read finally crossed outside the entire allocated WASM heap.

**Fix**: a single `if (vis->patch < 0) return;` at the top of `R_DrawVisSprite`, before `W_CacheLumpNum` is ever called — skip drawing that one sprite for that one frame, same "ignore bad data, don't crash" precedent as the v5 fix and the missing-mask fix above.

**Verified for real**, methodically, at every stage: (1) the debug build with all 6 fixes ran cleanly for 25 continuous seconds (vs. crashing at ~3s on every single previous attempt) with steadily growing WS packet activity throughout, confirmed via Playwright against a local static server; (2) a full clean production rebuild (`configure.ac` flags reverted to the original `ASSERTIONS=0 -s SAFE_HEAP=0 -s STACK_OVERFLOW_CHECK=1 -O3`, no debug symbols) — confirmed byte-identical-sized output to the documented v5 baseline (7.7MB raw) before `wasm-opt -Oz --all-features --strip-debug --strip-producers` brought it to 2.32MB, matching the documented v5 target — re-ran the same 25-second test against this production build locally, zero crash, *more* activity than the debug build (faster without SAFE_HEAP's instrumentation overhead); (3) uploaded as `games/doom/v6/` and re-ran the identical 25-second test directly against the live BunnyCDN URL, zero crash, confirmed via screenshot showing real, correctly-rendered E1M1 gameplay (HUD, weapon, level geometry all intact).

**Deployed:** `games/doom/v6/` on BunnyCDN. `DoomGame.jsx`'s `DOOM_BASE_URL` now points at `v6` (`v1`-`v5` abandoned per the same stuck-edge-cache convention as before).

**Lesson for next time this class of bug shows up**: when chasing a WASM out-of-bounds crash, don't stop at "is the destination/index in bounds" — also check whether the *source* lookup that produced the pointer/index in the first place was ever validated. Every fix in this investigation except the last one assumed the corruption was in screen-coordinate math; the real corruption was one level higher, in a frame-lookup result that had its own internal "not found" sentinel that nothing downstream ever checked for.

### DOOM — controls popup, mobile-gated (added 2026-06-27)
`DoomGame.jsx` now shows a dismissible "Controls" popup (WASD/arrows to move, Space to fire, E to use, Shift to run, 1-7 weapons, Tab map) once the iframe loads, host only — the spectator never receives input. Gated off on real touch devices via the same `('ontouchstart' in window) && navigator.maxTouchPoints > 0` check the wewatch-shell build itself uses to decide whether to render its own on-screen joystick/buttons, so the popup never shows where it'd be redundant. Confirmed via a real desktop-vs-resized-browser test: resizing a desktop browser window to "mobile view" has no real touch capability, so it correctly falls through to the keyboard popup — this is expected, not a bug, and is the same distinction that matters for Stellar Swarm below.

### Taskbar — minimize to draggable square during active games (added 2026-06-27)
User reported the taskbar pill sits fixed over a game's own bottom controls. Added: a small arrow button at the top-left corner of the pill (`Taskbar.jsx`, only rendered when `currentGame` is truthy) that collapses the full pill into a small (52px) draggable square using the same gradient background. The square tracks pointer drag (clamped to viewport bounds) and a tap (movement under 4px) restores the full pill. `isMinimized` resets to `false` automatically whenever `currentGame` becomes falsy, so leaving a game always restores the full taskbar.

### Stellar Swarm — controls popup, mobile-gated (added 2026-06-27)
Same pattern as DOOM's popup, added to `ShooterGame.jsx`. The real finding here: Stellar Swarm's desktop keyboard scheme is **EDSF, not WASD** (`game.js`: forward=E, backward=D, strafeLeft=S, strafeRight=F, rollLeft=W, rollRight=R, strafeUp=Backspace, strafeDown=Delete, boost=Shift, fire=left click, aim=mouse) — genuinely non-obvious, so the popup matters more here than for DOOM. Confirmed mobile touch controls (`client/src/touch-controls.js` in the space-shooter repo) already exist and work correctly — tested by loading the live Railway URL directly with real touch-device emulation (not a resized browser) and confirmed the move/aim joysticks and FIRE button render and function. The user's "mobile doesn't show controls" report was the same resized-desktop-browser testing artifact as DOOM's case, not a real bug. Popup is gated off on real touch devices the same way.
Known, unconfirmed-but-plausible follow-up (not fixed, flagged for if a real "joystick felt unresponsive" report ever comes in): the taskbar pill's footprint (up to 95vw wide, bottom ~20-90px) geometrically overlaps where the move/aim joystick zones sit (bottom 55% height, left/right 45% width each) on a real phone. The taskbar's shortened 1s auto-hide (see above) reduces but doesn't eliminate the window where it could intercept a touch meant for a joystick.

### Stream from Device — BunnyCDN CORS gap on Vercel (found/fixed 2026-06-27)
User reported "Stream from Device" worked on localhost but on Vercel never loaded duration/poster, and the whole upload seemed to never complete (leading to a stale "resume upload?" prompt on the next attempt). Root cause confirmed via the browser console: every `.ts` segment request was blocked by the browser — `No 'Access-Control-Allow-Origin' header is present` — not a code bug. BunnyCDN's pull zone (`letswatchout.b-cdn.net`) has "Add CORS Headers" enabled, but that feature only adds the header to file extensions in an explicit allowlist, and the list (set up before HLS streaming existed) didn't include `ts` or `m3u8`. The chunk uploads themselves were succeeding the whole time (confirmed in the pasted log) — only playback was blocked, since hls.js fetches segments via XHR (CORS-mandatory) unlike a plain `<video src>` tag. **Fix was a BunnyCDN dashboard change, not code**: added `ts, m3u8` to the CORS extension allowlist (Pull Zone → Headers → CORS). Worth remembering for any future new file extension introduced into the HLS/streaming pipeline — it needs to be added to this same allowlist or it'll silently fail with the exact same CORS error, on Vercel only (never reproduces on localhost, since the dev proxy makes same-origin requests).

### Stream from Device — adaptive chunk-upload concurrency (added 2026-06-27)
Separately, the user noted Vercel uploads felt slower than localhost. Root cause: `getUploadConcurrency()` (`uploadChunker.js`) picks concurrency from `navigator.connection.effectiveType`, and 3G previously meant fully serialized (concurrency=1) — added specifically as a "Railway proxy timeout fix" for genuinely slow connections. But `effectiveType` is a well-known-unreliable heuristic that frequently misclassifies a perfectly normal connection, especially right after page load with little RTT history — confirmed in the user's own pasted log, which showed batches of size 1 ("chunks 301-301 of 1170") on what was very likely a fine connection. Fix: raised the 3G floor from 1 to 2, and made `uploadChunksParallel` genuinely adaptive — tracks per-batch retries via a new `onRetry` callback on `uploadChunkWithRetry`; any retry within a batch backs concurrency off by 1 immediately (preserves the original timeout-safety intent), two consecutive clean batches ramp it up by 1, bounded to [1, 6]. This replaces the old "guess once from effectiveType, never adjust" model with one that self-corrects from what's actually happening on the wire.

### Stream from Device — "Browse Files" button state now matches playback readiness (added 2026-06-27)
Button text/disabled state was tied to `uploading` (the whole background chunk-transfer duration), not to whether playback had actually started (`isUploadReady`, from Phase 11). Fixed: text reverts to "Browse Files" and the button re-enables as soon as `isUploadReady` flips, even though chunks may still be sending in the background. Clicking it again while a background upload is still in flight now shows a toast ("Media is still loading in the background — please wait for it to finish") instead of silently racing a second upload or leaving the button looking enabled with no explanation.

### Voice notes — fixed broken production playback + Railway disk persistence risk (added 2026-06-27)
User reported a real `NotSupportedError: Failed to load because no supported source was found` testing a voice note on Vercel. Root cause: both `UploadVoiceNote` (`room_handlers.go`, room chat) and `UploadLobbyChatVoiceNoteHandler` (`lobby_chat_voice_notes.go`, DM/lobby chat) saved voice notes directly to Railway's **local disk** and returned a bare relative URL (`/uploads/room_media/voice_notes/...`) — never went through BunnyCDN at all, unlike every other media type in this app. On localhost this "works" because Vite's dev proxy forwards `/uploads/*` to the backend; on Vercel there's no such proxy, so the browser resolved the URL against the *frontend's* own origin and got back a non-audio response. Same bug class as the Phase 19 late-joiner URL fix, just never caught for voice notes. Also a real, independent data-loss risk: Railway's local filesystem isn't guaranteed to survive a redeploy.
**Fix**: both handlers still save locally first (unchanged — keeps local dev and the existing `delete_all_room_media.go` cleanup tool's directory-structure assumptions intact), then push to BunnyCDN via the already-established `utils.UploadLocalFileToBunnyCDN(localPath, remotePath, contentType)` helper, which transparently returns the relative path unchanged in local dev and an absolute CDN URL in production — same pattern already used by the device-streaming pipeline. No frontend changes needed for the URL itself. Also cleaned up a `Content-Type: 'multipart/form-data'` (no boundary) anti-pattern in the room voice-note upload call (`RoomPageNew.jsx`), matching the project's own documented convention of leaving it `undefined` so the browser fills in the boundary.

### GameLobbyModal — poster carousel redesign (added 2026-06-27)
Replaced the old vertical list-of-cards game picker with a poster carousel modeled on `CommunityEventsCard.jsx`'s fan pattern (a much closer match than `PricingModal.jsx`'s simpler fixed-4-card carousel): centered game at full scale, two neighbors fanning out and dimming on each side, real touch-drag swipe (mirrors `CommunityEventsCard`'s touchstart/move/end handling) plus chevron arrows, dot indicators. Title sits above the poster, description + min/max player count below it — only the centered card shows text, neighbors are bare posters. A search bar filters the carousel down to matches and auto-centers on the top one; clearing the search restores the full list and keeps the previous selection centered if it's still present (a `prevSelectedIdRef` + reconciliation effect, not a hard reset to index 0 every time).
Player-selection list, arcade single-player block, and footer buttons are unchanged structurally, just visually compacted.
**Mobile-fit follow-up, same day**: user asked for the whole modal (header through footer) to fit in one mobile viewport with zero scrolling, poster staying big. Fixed by: tightening padding throughout (header `p-6`→`px-4 py-3`-ish responsive scale, search bar moved closer to header, smaller text/icon sizes via `sm:` breakpoints), and converting the player-selection list from a single vertical column to a `grid-cols-2 sm:grid-cols-1` grid so it uses width instead of height on mobile. Header also changed to a `bg-gradient-to-r from-purple-600 to-blue-600` banner (matching `PricingModal.jsx`'s header pattern) to visually separate it from the dark modal body.
**Real bug found while verifying on a real mobile viewport**: the dot indicators rendered as large circles instead of the intended tiny pills, despite explicit small `w-*/h-*` classes. Root cause: a global min-button-size CSS rule (the same one `PricingModal.jsx`'s own pagination dots already had to work around) overrides small explicit button dimensions unless countered with `!min-h-0 !min-w-0`. Fixed by adding that same override — confirmed via a real iPhone-13-viewport screenshot before/after, this was a pre-existing app-wide gotcha, not new breakage. **Takeaway for any future small `<button>`-as-dot/indicator**: always add `!min-h-0 !min-w-0` up front rather than discovering it's needed after the fact.
Verified end-to-end via a temporary `/dev/game-lobby-test` route (mirroring the existing `/dev/svg-comparison` dev-tools pattern) rendering the modal with mock room members — screenshotted desktop default view, search-filtered view, chevron-navigated view, and a real iPhone-13-emulated mobile view; all confirmed correct, then the temp route/component were removed.
**Follow-up, same day**: chevron arrows weren't vertically centered on mobile — same `!min-h-0 !min-w-0` global min-button-size gotcha as the dots, just never applied to them since they used `p-1.5` padding instead of explicit `w-*/h-*`. Fixed by giving both chevrons an explicit `w-7 h-7 sm:w-8 sm:h-8` size + the same override. Also bumped the header icon/title size (`w-5 h-5`→`w-6 h-6`, `text-lg`→`text-xl` on mobile) per request — confirmed via the same mobile screenshot harness that the larger header didn't push the modal back into needing to scroll.

### Othello + Checkers — first two of the planned "lightweight 2D board games" wave (added 2026-06-27)

Following a discussion about adding popular, lightweight board/card games (Othello, Checkers, Go, Shogi, Monopoly, generic "card games") as cheap volume alongside the existing turn-based games, built the two cheapest ones first using the exact established pattern: a `processXMove(gameState, playerID, moveData) (gameOver bool, winnerID *uint, err error)` function in `backend/internal/handlers/games/`, wired into `game_manager.go`'s `ProcessMove` switch and `initializeGameState` switch, plus a React component matching `TicTacToeGame.jsx`'s prop shape (`gameState, players, currentUserId, onMove, onClose, onEndGame`).

**Othello** (`othello.go` + `OthelloGame.jsx`): 64-cell flat board (`[]string`, "" / "B" / "W"), standard 4-disc opening. Bracket-and-flip logic mirrors the textbook algorithm: for each of 8 directions from the placed disc, walk outward collecting opponent discs, flip them if the walk terminates on one of your own. Auto-pass handling is the one non-obvious part: when the *opponent* has no legal move, `processOthelloMove` decrements `CurrentTurn` by one before returning, which cancels out `ProcessMove`'s own unconditional `(CurrentTurn+1) % len(Players)` advance afterward — net effect is the same player goes again, skipping the stuck opponent, without `ProcessMove` itself needing any othello-specific logic. Game ends (most discs wins) when *neither* side has a legal move. Frontend mirrors the exact same flip/legal-move JS functions for instant client-side legal-move highlighting and pass-button gating, with the server re-validating everything regardless.

**Checkers** (`checkers.go` + `CheckersGame.jsx`): standard American rules — 32 dark squares, men move/capture forward-only, kings (reaching the back row) move either diagonal direction, mandatory capture (if any of your pieces can jump anywhere on the board, simple moves are rejected), and forced multi-jump continuation via `gameState.GameData["must_continue_from"]` (same turn-cancel trick as Othello's pass-skip, so the same player keeps moving until no further jump is available from that piece). Win condition: opponent has zero pieces, or zero legal moves (stalemate loss, standard checkers rule).

**Real bug #1**: `GameSessionState.GameData` is a *fresh, empty* runtime map created in `StartGame` (`GameData: make(map[string]interface{})`) — it is never seeded from `initializeGameState`'s return value, which only ever reaches the DB-persisted `GameSession.GameState` field and the initial `game_started` broadcast. `tic_tac_toe.go` and `chess.go` both already work around this with a lazy-init fallback inside their own move-processing function (build the board/FEN right there if `GameData["board"]` is absent) rather than assuming it's pre-populated — `othello.go`/`checkers.go` initially assumed the latter and failed every single first move with "board not initialized," caught immediately by an actual WS test rather than by reading the code. Fixed by adding the identical lazy-init fallback, and extracted `othelloInitialBoard()`/`checkersInitialBoard()` as free functions shared between `initializeGameState`'s case and the fallback, so the two paths can't drift out of sync with each other.

**Real bug #2, in checkers.go specifically**: `checkersClassifyMove`'s original signature `(isJump bool, capturedIndex int)` couldn't actually distinguish "legal simple move, no capture" from "illegal move" — both legitimately return `capturedIndex == -1`, and the original code returned `(false, -1)` from *both* branches of the single-step-diagonal check. The caller's `if captured == -1 && !isJump { return error("illegal move") }` therefore rejected every single legal simple move in the game, full stop — caught the same way, via a real WS test attempting an ordinary forward move. Fixed by adding a third `valid bool` return value, with the two branches now correctly returning `(true, false, -1)` for a legal move and `(false, false, -1)` for an illegal one.

**Late-join rehydration** — the user's explicit requirement going into this work — needed *zero* new code for either game: `GetActiveGameMessage(roomID)` (`websocket_handler.go`) is entirely generic, driven by whatever `GameManager.GetActiveGame` returns regardless of game type, and fires automatically as part of the WS connection handshake whenever a `session_id` query param is present (`JoinWatchSession`, called inline during the WS upgrade, not a separate message type). Both games inherit this for free by virtue of using the same `GameSessionState`/`GameManager` machinery as every other game.

**Verified for real, end-to-end, via raw WebSocket test clients** (not the UI — faster to drive precisely and to inspect exact board state byte-for-byte): three real throwaway accounts, a real room, a real watch session. Othello: host placed a disc, confirmed the correct bracket-flip applied server-side, then a fresh connection (genuinely new account, not a reconnect) joined afterward and received a rehydration board that matched the host's post-move board exactly. Checkers: black simple move, red simple move (sent from the actual red account — sending it from black's connection first surfaced a correct "not your turn" rejection, confirming turn enforcement works), black jump-captures red (confirmed the captured square cleared and the jumping piece landed correctly), red makes another simple move while no capture is available (confirming mandatory-capture doesn't wrongly block non-capture turns when no capture exists anywhere), then a genuinely separate third account joined late and received the exact correct current board. Frontend: both components screenshotted standalone via a temporary dev route — Othello showed the correct green board, legal-move dots, and disc counts; Checkers showed the correct brown/tan board, standard 12-piece starting position, and yellow selectable-piece rings. All temporary test accounts, the test room, and test scripts were deleted afterward.

**Go (the board game) — evaluated, not built**: per the earlier discussion, [`acityinohio/baduk`](https://github.com/acityinohio/baduk) is a real, dedicated Go-language library for the board game Go with scoring support. A quick look found no mention of ko-rule handling in its docs, and it's a small project (35 commits) — not yet confirmed solid enough to build on. Needs a proper read of the actual source (not just the README) before deciding whether it meaningfully reduces Go's cost relative to Shogi, which has no Go-language library at all and would need to be fully hand-built.

**Game poster images moved to BunnyCDN (same day)**: all 8 game posters (the 6 pre-existing ones plus Othello/Checkers) were moved from `frontend/public/images/` to BunnyCDN at `games/posters/{id}.webp`, on the same CDN origin `DoomGame.jsx`/`ShooterGame.jsx` already hardcode for their own assets — keeps the (growing) poster collection out of the deployed frontend bundle entirely, per explicit request. `GameLobbyModal.jsx`'s `image` fields and the backend's `gamePosterURL()` (`websocket_handler.go`) both now build full CDN URLs via a shared base-URL constant instead of `/images/...` paths. No changes needed to the frontend's `resolvePreviewUrl` (`LobbyPage.jsx`) — it already short-circuits and passes through any `http(s)://` URL unchanged; the `/images/` special-case in that function is now simply unused by game posters specifically (left in place, since other non-game frontend-relative assets may still rely on it). Verified for real: a live page load showed zero failed CDN requests and all posters rendering correctly in the carousel; the local `.webp` files were then deleted from `frontend/public/images/`. (Note: this also retroactively makes the "custom poster at `/images/stellarswarm.webp`" line in the Stellar Swarm section below stale — its poster moved to the CDN along with everything else; not re-stated there to avoid editing around a past entry.)

### Crazy Eights — first card game; hidden-hand architecture for the whole future card-game collection (added 2026-06-27)

First of the planned multiplayer card game collection (after Othello/Checkers proved out the lightweight-board-game pattern). Picked as the simplest real card game to prove the pattern: no bidding, no partnerships, no meld validation — match the discard pile's rank or suit, 8s are wild, first to empty their hand wins.

**The one genuinely new architectural problem**: every prior game in this package (tic_tac_toe, chess, othello, checkers, trivia) is perfect-information — the whole board is public, so broadcasting full `GameData` to the whole room via `BroadcastJSON` is fine. A card game can't work that way: a player's hand, and the draw pile's order, are the entire game's hidden information. Broadcasting them would leak everything to every other player and spectator.

**Solution — split state into public and private:**
- `GameSessionState` (`game_manager.go`) gained three new fields — `Hands map[uint][]string`, `DrawPile []string`, `DiscardPile []string` — deliberately declared *outside* `GameData` and never read into it. Only non-revealing derivatives (`hand_counts` keyed by user ID string, `draw_pile_count`, `discard_top`, `current_suit`) get mirrored into `GameData` by `syncCrazyEightsPublicState` (`crazy_eights.go`) — these are the only things about hands/draw-pile safe to broadcast.
- Each player's actual hand reaches them via a new **private, single-user message** — `hub.BroadcastToUser` (already existed, used by LiveShare) — never a room broadcast. Needed a new `Hub.BroadcastJSONToUser(userID, roomID, data map[string]interface{})` (`websocket.go`) as the JSON-only counterpart, mirroring how `BroadcastJSON` already wraps `BroadcastToRoom` — the `games` package can't import `handlers.OutgoingMessage` (import-cycle avoidance, same `MessageHub interface{}` pattern documented elsewhere in this file), so the wrapper exists specifically so a plain interface assertion against built-in types is enough.
- New `GameWebSocketHandler.GetPlayerHandMessage(roomID, userID)` / `.sendHandUpdate(roomID, userID)` (`websocket_handler.go`): looks up the hand via `GameManager.GetPlayerHand`, returns nil (no-op) for any non-card-game or non-player. Called from three places: once per player right after the `game_started` broadcast (`handleGameStart`), once for the acting player right after `BroadcastGameState` on every move (`handleGameMove` — a play removes a card, a draw adds one), and from `websocket.go`'s existing late-join rehydration block (right after the generic `GetActiveGameMessage` call) for **any** reconnecting/late-joining client, player or not — a non-player gets nil and nothing is sent.
- Frontend: `VideoWatch.jsx` gained a new `myHand` state, set by a new `hand_update` case in the existing game-message switch, reset alongside `activeGame` on close. Passed through `GameOverlay.jsx` as a new `myHand` prop to `CrazyEightsGame.jsx` only.

**Dealing happens at game start, not lazily on first move** — unlike every other game here, which lazily initializes `GameData["board"]` on the first move that touches it (since `GameData` starts as a fresh empty map, never seeded from `initializeGameState`'s return value — a gap documented in the Othello/Checkers section above). A card game can't defer this: players need to see their hand from the very first moment, before anyone has moved. `dealCrazyEights` is called directly from `StartGame`, gated by game type.

**Real bug found and fixed while wiring this up**: `dealCrazyEights` only mutates `gameState.GameData` (the runtime copy) — but the *initial* `game_started` broadcast (`handleGameStart`) reads `gameSession.GameState` (the DB-row snapshot `initializeGameState` populated *before* dealing ran). Every other game's `initializeGameState` case populates `GameState` directly, so they never hit this gap; crazy_eights needed an explicit `gameState.GameSession.GameState = gameState.GameData` sync right after dealing (the exact same reconciliation `ProcessMove` already does after every move, just also needed once at creation time). Without this fix, the very first broadcast would have carried an empty `game_state` (no `discard_top`/`hand_counts`) for crazy_eights specifically.

**Game logic** (`cards.go` + `crazy_eights.go`): cards are plain `"<rank><suit>"` strings (`"8S"`, `"10H"`) — same plain-string-state approach `othello.go`/`checkers.go` already use for board cells, no custom type needed. `NewDeck()`/`ShuffleDeck()` are deliberately generic (not Crazy-Eights-specific) since every future card game in the collection will need the same deck/shuffle primitives. The discard pile's *first* card can never be an 8 (skipped forward past any 8s when flipping the starting card — nobody has had a turn yet to name the wild suit it would require). Draw-pile exhaustion reshuffles the discard pile (minus its current top card, which must stay visible) into a fresh draw pile — standard Crazy Eights rule. Win condition: hand reaches zero cards.

**Verified for real, end-to-end, via raw WebSocket test clients** (three real throwaway accounts, a real room, a real watch session — same methodology as Othello/Checkers): dealt hands confirmed 7 cards each with zero cross-leak between players' own `hand_update` messages; a draw correctly grew the acting player's hand by one and advanced the turn; a play correctly shrank the hand by one and updated the public `discard_top`; the wild-8 mechanic confirmed two ways — playing an 8 with `next_suit: "H"` correctly set `current_suit` to the chosen suit, and a subsequent card matching neither the new suit nor rank 8 was confirmed rejected (`"card doesn't match the current rank or suit"`, verified directly in the backend log) by a different player on their turn. **Late-join rehydration — the user's explicit requirement going into the board-games work, re-verified here for the hidden-information case specifically** — tested two distinct scenarios: a genuinely separate spectator account (never a player) correctly received the public `game_started` rehydration and *zero* `hand_update` messages; a player listed in `start_game`'s payload who simply hadn't opened a WS connection yet (the realistic "slower to load the room than the host who started the game" case) correctly received *both* the public state and their own correct 7-card hand on connecting, with no cross-leak in either direction.

**Scope note, not a bug**: disconnecting a player from a 2-player game forfeits it immediately and unconditionally (`GameManager.HandlePlayerDisconnect`) — this is pre-existing, uniform behavior across *every* game type in this package (tic_tac_toe, chess, othello, checkers, all the same), confirmed via the same duplicate-connection-cleanup code path (`websocket.go`) that triggered it during testing. There is no "reconnect within N seconds without forfeiting" grace period for any game today. The hand-rehydration work above covers the *actually reachable* late-join case (never-yet-connected, joining a still-active game) — it does not and was not intended to add a reconnect grace period, which would be a separate, larger change affecting every game type, not just card games.

### Ludo — finished a years-old half-built stub; first 2-4 player dice game (added 2026-06-27)

Slotted in as a quick, lightweight win before tackling the much heavier Quake3/OpenArena port (see below). Package research (Go and JS) found nothing reusable for the rules engine — the one real Go candidate (`fareez-ahamed/ludo`) is just type definitions, no actual movement/capture/turn logic — so this was hand-built, same as Othello/Checkers. One genuine asset find: a CC-BY-4.0 Ludo board/token/dice pack exists on OpenGameArt, but it was skipped in favor of a hand-built CSS board for v1, consistent with every other game's zero-external-dependency approach and to avoid new integration risk on a "reduce the lift" pick.

**Found a years-old abandoned stub already sitting in the codebase.** `game_manager.go` already had `initializeLudoBoard(playerCount)` (called from `initializeGameState`'s `"ludo"` case) and `Player.Color`/`GameType` comments mentioning Ludo — but no `ludo.go`, no `processLudoMove`, and `"ludo"` was never in `StartGame`'s allowlist. A stale planning doc (`GAME_SYSTEM_REVIEW.md`, predating this session's entire game roster — it references Snake/Tetris, which don't exist anywhere in the current `GameLobbyModal.jsx`) describes this as "Phase 4, post-launch." The stub's exact field shape (`board.tokens[color][].{id,position,safe}`) wasn't reused as-is — replaced with a cleaner shape (`tokens[color][].{id,position}`, no redundant `safe` field, computed on demand instead) since nothing else in the codebase ever read the old shape.

**Ludo is perfect-information** (unlike Crazy Eights) — captured pieces and every token's position are visible to both players at all times, no piece stand of hidden cards. So this uses the plain `GameData`-only pattern Chess/Othello/Checkers already established; none of Crazy Eights' private-hand plumbing was needed.

**Position convention**: each token's position is a single int relative to its own color's entry point: `-1` = in base/yard, `0`-`50` = the 51-square shared outer track (so a token needs exactly 57 forward steps total — 51 shared + 6 home-column cells — from entry to home), `51`-`56` = that color's own colored home-column stretch, `57` = home/finished. Colors are fixed `red, blue, green, yellow` at entry offsets `0, 13, 26, 39` (evenly spaced around the 52-square loop) — same index-derived-from-`Players[]`-order convention Othello already uses for Black/White, so no separate "which color am I" field needs to be broadcast. Safe squares (no capture possible) are each color's own entry plus one "star" square roughly opposite it: `{0, 8, 13, 21, 26, 34, 39, 47}`.

**Two-phase turn flow — genuinely new shape for this package.** Every other game here is "one message = one complete move." Ludo needs *roll, then move*: a player sends `roll_dice` (server picks 1-6, checks whether any of their tokens can legally use it), and only if a legal move exists do they then send `move_token` with the token index to act on. `GameData.awaiting_move`/`current_dice` track this phase, and `ProcessMove`'s generic "not your turn" check (confirmed correct via direct backend-log inspection during testing — a test script's own false-negative had initially suggested otherwise, same class of timing artifact as the Crazy Eights wild-8 test) applies to both halves of a turn.

**Same-player-continues uses the established turn-decrement trick three separate times in this one game** — more than any prior game needed: (1) after `roll_dice` finds a legal move (the same player must now send `move_token` — the turn hasn't actually changed yet), (2) after `roll_dice` rolls a 6 with *no* legal move available (six always grants a re-roll even with nothing to use it on), and (3) after `move_token` when the roll was a 6, captured an opponent, or reached home (any one of which grants a bonus turn — the generous "Ludo King" variant, matching what the user referenced by name). Three 6s rolled consecutively forfeits that roll's move and passes the turn (standard anti-stalling rule) — tracked via `consecutive_sixes`, which needs no explicit reset when a turn genuinely passes to a different player: that only happens after a non-6 roll, and `processLudoRoll` already resets the counter to 0 on every non-6 roll regardless of whose turn it is.

**Deliberately simplified, not a bug**: no "blockade" rule (two same-color tokens stacked on one square blocking opponents) — same-color tokens can freely coexist on a square with no special effect. A genuine rule-set choice to keep v1 lighter, not an oversight.

**Defensive fix**: `ludoColors` only has 4 entries — `StartGame` now explicitly rejects more than 4 players for `"ludo"` specifically (every other game here is implicitly capped by its own move logic, so this check didn't exist anywhere generically and would otherwise panic on an out-of-bounds slice index).

**Frontend board built and verified geometrically before wiring up gameplay** — the classic 15x15 cross-shaped board's 51-cell shared path was generated programmatically (4 straight segments + corner cells) rather than hand-typed as one 52-tuple list, then checked with a standalone script for: exactly 51 unique cells, no out-of-bounds or center-3x3 collisions, and correct 13-cell entry spacing between colors. A few "non-adjacent" transitions turned up at the 4 corners closest to the center (a diagonal gap around the excluded center 3x3) — confirmed cosmetically irrelevant since tokens jump directly to their new cell on each state update with no sliding/interpolated animation, so no continuous path-walking is ever rendered. A real screenshot of the rendered board (3-player test data) confirmed a correctly recognizable Ludo board: colored yards only for active colors, correctly colored home columns converging on the center, star/safe squares highlighted, and the one legal token correctly pulsing/highlighted for the test's pending roll.

**Verified for real, end-to-end, via raw WebSocket test clients** (three real throwaway accounts, a real room, a real watch session): initial deal confirmed all 8-12 tokens start at `-1`; out-of-turn move rejection confirmed directly in the backend log (`"not your turn"`) after the test script's own check first reported a false negative; a 2-player run over 60 rounds of adaptive random play confirmed leaving base on a 6, ordinary forward movement, extra turns on 6 (visible as several consecutive same-color moves in the log), a token correctly reaching its home column, and correct late-join rehydration (a genuinely fresh third account received public state matching the live board exactly). A separate 3-player, 200-round run produced **8 real captures** with consistent resulting state each time, and correctly never declared a winner despite one color reaching 3-of-4 tokens home by the test's end — confirming the "all 4 home" win condition isn't triggering early. The three-6s anti-stall rule wasn't naturally observed in either run (statistically rare enough that it didn't come up — would need a much longer run to reliably hit 3 consecutive 6s for one player) and is covered by code review rather than a live trigger, the same standard applied to a handful of low-risk paths in earlier games this session.

Poster: placeholder (`games/posters/crazy_eights.webp` on BunnyCDN, per the same convention as Othello/Checkers) pending custom art.

### Space Shooter ("Stellar Swarm") — first true-simultaneous-multiplayer 3D game, VideoWatch-exclusive (added 2026-06-25)

User-facing display name is **"Stellar Swarm"** (`GameLobbyModal.jsx`'s `name` field, custom poster now on BunnyCDN per the section above, same path reused by the backend's `gamePosterURL()` lobby-preview mapping) — internal identifiers (`id`/`game_type`: `space_shooter`, the `ShooterGame.jsx` filename, the Railway service name, the `arcadeGameTypes`/`minPlayersOverride` map keys) intentionally stay `space_shooter` throughout the code, same pattern as any product where the marketing name differs from the internal codename. Per-match player cap stays at **8** (confirmed correct, not changed) — deliberately conservative while the feature is new; raise later once proven at scale, same reasoning as the Railway service's `MAX_PLAYERS`/`PLAYERS_PER_WORLD` env vars.

Following DOOM's solo-arcade model, the user wanted genuine simultaneous multiplayer (multiple real players in the same match, not host+spectators) to reinforce the platform's "meet and hang out together" value. First of three candidates scoped (racing game and Quake/OpenArena to follow later, one at a time). Built on `nickyvanurk/3d-multiplayer-browser-shooter` (MIT) — specifically its **`v3` branch**, not `master`: `master` is a newer `bitecs`-based rewrite with zero combat code (pure flying demo); `v3` (older, `ecsy`-based) has the real feature set — health, weapons, bullets, collision, aim-assist. Branch chosen only after grepping `master`'s full tree for `shoot|weapon|health|damage` and finding nothing.

**Architecture — genuinely separate deployed service, not folded into WeWatch's backend.** Unlike DOOM (whose netcode is tunneled through WeWatch's own WS relay since the engine has no native multiplayer transport), this game already has its own working client-server architecture (Node.js + `ws` + Express + `ecsy` ECS + `ammo.js` physics, server-authoritative). Deployed as its own Railway service (`space-shooter`) inside the existing `agile-nourishment` project, alongside the `WeWatch` backend and `Postgres` services already there — same Railway account/project the user already manages, not a new provider. WeWatch's Go backend's only role is the same one it already plays for Trivia/TicTacToe: announce a match started and who's in it (`GameSession.GameState` stays an empty `{}` for this game — all real game state lives entirely in the Railway service's own ECS world).

**The one real upstream change — per-room match isolation.** Originally, `server/src/index.js` pre-allocated N fixed `World` instances at boot and assigned new connections to "the first non-full world" — no concept of a specific room. Replaced with lazy per-room `World` creation, keyed by a `?room=<id>` query param the client now passes on its WS URL (`client/src/connection.js`): server (`server/src/server.js`) extracts it via `new URL(req.url, 'http://localhost').searchParams.get('room')` and attaches it to the connection object; `server/src/index.js` looks up or creates a `Map`-backed world per room id, routing connections to it. A `World.onEmpty(callback)` hook (mirroring the existing `onDisconnect` pattern already used throughout this codebase) fires when `connectedClients` hits 0, destroying the world (`clearInterval` on its 60Hz update loop) and removing it from the map — without this, every room that ever played a match would leave an empty world ticking forever.

**Real bug found and fixed: a race specific to *lazy* world creation, invisible in the original upfront-allocation design.** `World`'s constructor registers its ECS systems (including `NetworkMessageSystem`, whose `connections.added` query is what wires up a player's disconnect handler) only *after* `Ammo()` (the WASM physics module) resolves — an async `.then()`. In the original code, all worlds were created at server boot, long before any real player ever connected, so `Ammo()` had always resolved by the time `handlePlayerConnect` ran. With lazy creation, the *first* player to join a brand-new room creates the entity in the same tick as the `World` itself — before `Ammo()` resolves and the systems exist — so that player's `connections.added` event was silently missed forever; their disconnect handler never got registered, so `connectedClients` never reached 0, so the room's world never got cleaned up. Found via direct instrumentation (logged `hasCallback` on every raw `ws close` event) showing the *first* player's disconnect cleanly fired the raw close event but `hasCallback=false`, while the *second* player's disconnect worked correctly (joined after `Ammo()` had resolved). Fixed with a `ready`/`pendingConnections` queue on `World`: `handlePlayerConnect` defers (queues) if the world isn't ready yet, and `Ammo().then(...)` flushes the queue once systems are actually registered — robust regardless of how many players race ahead of the async resolution, not just the first one.

**Touch controls (additive, zero changes to existing keyboard/mouse path).** The upstream game had zero mobile input support — confirmed by reading every listener in `input-system.js`. Aiming there is **absolute mouse position relative to screen center** (not pointer-lock delta) feeding `input.aim.mouse.{x,y}` (range -1..1), which the server's `spaceship-controller-system.js` uses directly as yaw/pitch *angular velocity* — i.e. aim position *is* the turn-rate input, a flight-stick model, not an independent camera look. This made touch mapping straightforward: `client/src/touch-controls.js` (new file, ~feature-detected via `isTouchDevice()`) adds two `nipplejs` virtual joysticks — left (`forward`/`backward`/`strafeLeft`/`strafeRight`, dead-zone 0.15) and right (writes directly into the same `input.aim.mouse`, matching the desktop range exactly) — plus a dedicated tap-and-hold FIRE button (`weaponPrimary`). Roll and vertical strafe stay keyboard/mouse-only — deliberately left off the touch UI; 6 inputs on a small screen would hurt more than help for a casual mobile player. **Pinned `nipplejs@0.10.2`**, not the current `1.0.4` — the newer version's pre-built `dist` bundle uses modern syntax (`Object.hasOwn`, optional chaining) this project's webpack 4 parser can't handle even with `node_modules` excluded from babel-loader (a parse-time failure, not a transform one); 0.10.2 predates that rewrite and is plain ES5/ES2015. Verified via Playwright with CDP's `Input.dispatchTouchEvent` (not hand-rolled `TouchEvent` construction, which `nipplejs`'s internal identifier-tracking didn't reliably respond to) — confirmed the move stick's vector correctly maps `y>0` (drag up) to `forward=true`, the aim stick correctly updates `input.aim.mouse`, and the fire button correctly toggles `weaponPrimary` on press/release.

**Real backend gap found during WeWatch-side wiring: the generic `start_game` WS handler's min-player check didn't know this game could be played solo.** `games/websocket_handler.go` hardcoded `minPlayers := 2` for any non-arcade game type, relaxed to 1 only via the `arcadeGameTypes` map (currently just `doom`). Adding `space_shooter` to that map would have been semantically wrong — `arcadeGameTypes` specifically means "host is the only participant, ever" (still true and intentional for DOOM), not "also playable solo." Added a separate `minPlayersOverride map[string]int{"space_shooter": 1}` instead, checked only when the game isn't in `arcadeGameTypes` — keeps the two concepts (single-player-only vs. min-player-count) independent. Found by reading the actual rejection (`"at least 2 player(s) required"`) surfaced during real end-to-end testing with only the host selected as a player.

**WeWatch frontend wiring:**
- `frontend/src/components/Games/ShooterGame.jsx` — iframe wrapper mirroring `DoomGame.jsx`'s shape, but simpler: no relay-packet plumbing at all (this game's netcode talks directly from the iframe to the Railway WS server; WeWatch's backend never touches a single packet, unlike DOOM's relay). `src` is `https://space-shooter-production-6e0d.up.railway.app/?room=<roomId>` — the WeWatch room id is passed straight through as the query param the server-side per-room routing (above) reads. `sandbox="allow-scripts allow-same-origin"` — `allow-same-origin` is required for `location.origin` to resolve to a real value inside the iframe (a fully sandboxed iframe gets an opaque `null` origin, which would break the client's own `ws://`/`wss://` host derivation); no `allow-pointer-lock` needed since this game never uses the Pointer Lock API (confirmed via the absolute-mouse-position aim model above). Every player (host included) gets an identical real player view — no host/spectator split, unlike DOOM.
- **Exit semantics differ from DOOM on purpose**, reflecting true multiplayer vs. host-authoritative-with-spectators: a plain X close just calls `onClose()` for *that* player only (others keep playing, like leaving a real multiplayer match) — never `onEndGame()`. Only the WeWatch room host sees an additional, explicit "End for Everyone" button that calls `onEndGame()` (ends the WeWatch `GameSession`, broadcasts `game_ended`) before closing — mirrors Trivia's host-only-end pattern rather than DOOM's "anyone's X ends it" pattern, since there's no natural 2-player forfeit-loses semantic for an N-player arena shooter.
- `GameOverlay.jsx` — new `case 'space_shooter':`, lazy-loaded (`React.lazy`, same as `DoomGame`), passes `roomId` through (new prop on `GameOverlay` itself, threaded from `VideoWatch.jsx`'s existing `roomId` from `useParams()` — `CinemaScene3DDemo.jsx` doesn't need it since its `GameOverlay` render is commented out, see below).
- `GameLobbyModal.jsx` — new entry, `type: 'multiplayer'` (fits the existing generic player-picker UI with zero special-casing, same as Trivia), `minPlayers: 1, maxPlayers: 8` (matches the Railway service's `PLAYERS_PER_WORLD=8` env var), `heavy: true` flag (new field, also added to the existing `doom` entry).
- **Real, pre-existing bug fixed in the same pass, confirmed via direct testing**: `GameLobbyModal` was reachable from `CinemaScene3DDemo.jsx` (3D Cinema) showing every game with zero filtering, but `CinemaScene3DDemo.jsx`'s own `GameOverlay` render is *commented out* (games show on the in-scene 3D screen via a separate `GameScreenRenderer` mechanism instead) — meaning DOOM was already selectable-but-silently-broken there before this change, and Space Shooter would have been too. Fixed with a new `allowHeavyGames` prop on `GameLobbyModal` (default `true`): `visibleGames = allowHeavyGames ? games : games.filter(g => !g.heavy)`. `VideoWatch.jsx` doesn't pass it (defaults `true` — heavy games allowed there, by design, per the "VideoWatch-exclusive" decision). `CinemaScene3DDemo.jsx` explicitly passes `allowHeavyGames={false}`. `LectureHallPage.jsx` doesn't import `GameLobbyModal` at all — already structurally unreachable there, confirmed via grep, no change needed.
- `frontend/src/utils/devicePerformance.js` — new shared `checkDevicePerformance()` (soft warning only, never blocking, per explicit product decision: target floor is "a mid-range phone that can handle PUBG Mobile," underpowered devices get a dismissible toast, never a hard gate). Heuristic: `navigator.hardwareConcurrency <= 2` or `navigator.deviceMemory <= 2` (where available) → `isLowEnd`. Dismissal persisted per-game in `sessionStorage`. Wired into `ShooterGame.jsx` only for now (DOOM already shipped without it; not retrofitted here to keep this change scoped) — written to be reusable as-is for the racing game and Quake/OpenArena later.
- Backend: `games/game_manager.go`'s game-type allowlist and `games/websocket_handler.go`'s `gamePosterURL()` (lobby session-preview poster mapping, → `/images/space-shooter.svg`, a small original SVG icon, same pattern as `doom.svg`) both needed the new `"space_shooter"` case added — the otherwise-true "zero backend Go changes needed for a new game type" claim from the DOOM work applies to `GameState` initialization (no case needed, falls through to an empty `{}`) but not to these two explicit allowlists.

**Verified for real, end-to-end, in the actual WeWatch app (not just against the bare Railway service):** real test account, real room, real watch session. Confirmed via direct WS/console log inspection: `GameLobbyModal` correctly shows "Space Shooter" (custom SVG poster rendering correctly) alongside the other 4 games; selecting it shows the correct `Min: 1 | Max: 8` from the real per-game config; starting it sends `start_game` → backend logs `🎮 [VideoWatch] Game started: {game_type: space_shooter, ...}` → `GameOverlay` renders `ShooterGame` → the iframe's actual `src` attribute is the exact expected `https://space-shooter-production-6e0d.up.railway.app/?room=195` → screenshotted the *real, loaded, rendering* 3D ship-and-starfield scene inside that iframe, embedded in the live WeWatch UI (taskbar, "End for Everyone" button all visible simultaneously) — not a mock or a standalone test page. Clicking "End for Everyone" correctly sent `end_game`, logged `🎮 [VideoWatch] Game ended: {..., reason: forfeited}`, and the overlay/iframe correctly unmounted. Separately confirmed via raw WS clients (bypassing the browser entirely) against the real deployed Railway service: two connections to the same room id land in the same world (see each other's asteroid-field spawn stream); a third connection to a different room id gets a fully separate world; both rooms' worlds correctly self-destruct (`"Room X is empty, destroying its world"`) once their last player disconnects, including under near-simultaneous multi-room disconnects.

**Known, accepted test-environment quirk (not a product bug):** the local Postgres `watch_sessions` row's `is_active` flag and the `/active-session` GET endpoint's own "is there a live session" check can disagree after an abrupt disconnect (e.g. closing a test browser without a clean WS close) — a session can be `is_active=true` in the DB (blocking a fresh `CreateWatchSession` call with "Active session already exists") while the GET check reports `is_existing:false`. Pre-existing, unrelated to this game integration; surfaced repeatedly during automated testing and worked around by ending the stale row directly via `POST /sessions/:id/end`. Not investigated further — flagged here in case it resurfaces during future testing.

### Stellar Swarm — root cause found and fixed: a single bug disabled ALL player input (movement, shooting, boost) for the entire session (fixed 2026-06-27, NOT YET DEPLOYED)

User reported three issues: bullets have no visible animation ("I can't see what is shot or what reacts"), no shooting sounds, and boost doesn't work. Investigated by reading the actual `nickyvanurk/3d-multiplayer-browser-shooter` source at `~/dev-tools/space-shooter` (separate repo from WeWatch, deployed as its own Railway service) rather than guessing.

**Sound — confirmed not a bug, never built.** Grepped the entire client for any audio API usage (`Audio`, `AudioContext`, `Howler`, `.play()`) — zero matches outside one unrelated HUD video element. The upstream game never shipped sound effects at all. Out of scope for a "fix"; would be new work.

**Bullets/boost — root-caused to a real, severe bug via local reproduction, not guesswork.** Initial static analysis (bullet speed vs. ship acceleration, camera FOV/distance, material overrides) all looked individually plausible but inconclusive — the upstream code already applies a bright orange `MeshBasicMaterial` override to bullets (`model-loading-system.js`), and the mesh-assignment pipeline (`webgl-renderer-system.js`) looked structurally correct on paper. Rather than keep guessing against the black-box deployed Railway instance, built and ran the game **locally** (`NODE_OPTIONS=--openssl-legacy-provider npx webpack --config client/webpack.prod.js`, then `PRODUCTION=true npm run server:start`) so debug instrumentation could be added directly to the source and iterated quickly — the same methodology already used for the DOOM WASM crash investigation.

Added temporary `console.log` instrumentation across the full input→network→render pipeline (client mousedown handler, WELCOME/addPlayer message handling, server-side weapon firing, bullet spawning, mesh-ready confirmation) and ran a real Playwright session holding fire for several seconds. Result: **zero bullets were ever created server-side, for the entire session, no matter how long fire was held** — confirmed via both browser console and server log (`LOG_LEVEL=debug` in `.env`), which showed 500 asteroids + the player's own spaceship spawning correctly, then *nothing* until disconnect.

Traced this to `server/src/systems/network-event-system.js`'s per-connection input-sequencing logic:
```js
if (connection.hasInputs()) {
  let input = connection.popInput();
  while (input && input.seq < connection.lastProcessedInput + 1) {
    input = connection.popInput();
  }
  if (!input) return;
  // ...apply input.data to the ECS Input component...
}
connection.lastProcessedInput++;  // ← ran unconditionally, every server tick
```
`connection.lastProcessedInput` incremented **once per server tick (~60Hz), unconditionally**, while the client's own input `seq` only increments once its fixed-timestep sender starts actually transmitting (after WS handshake + asset loading). Confirmed via direct logging: by the time the client's first real input arrived, `lastProcessedInput` was already **129 ticks ahead of `seq=0`** — and because the gap can only grow (server ticks unconditionally, `seq` can never catch up to an ever-incrementing counter it didn't create), `input.seq < connection.lastProcessedInput + 1` was true **forever after**. Every single input message — `forward`/`backward`/`strafeLeft`/`weaponPrimary`/`boost`/everything — was silently discarded as "too old," permanently, from essentially the start of every session. `aim` (mouse look) appeared to work because it's rendered client-side locally for the camera, independent of this broken server sync — explaining why the user could correctly report "follows the mouse" while shooting and boost were completely dead.

**Fix:** only advance `lastProcessedInput` to the `seq` of the input actually consumed, and only when one was found — never an unconditional per-tick increment:
```js
component.aim = aim;
connection.lastProcessedInput = input.seq;  // moved inside the `if`, replacing the unconditional `++` that used to sit after this block
```

**Verified for real, end-to-end, locally**, with the same Playwright methodology: after the fix, held fire and got real `[DEBUG-BULLET] created`/`mesh ready` log lines, then confirmed visually via screenshot — two bright orange-yellow bullet streaks clearly visible firing from the ship, receding correctly over subsequent frames. Separately verified movement and boost: a 1.5s forward-only flight screenshot vs. a 1.5s forward+boost screenshot showed the boosted ship had travelled dramatically farther into the (stationary) asteroid field — confirming the *same* fix also restored ordinary movement and the boost speed multiplier, not just shooting. This one bug had been silently disabling the entire core gameplay loop (movement, shooting, boost) for the whole lifetime of the feature, with only client-side aim/camera-look surviving.

All debug instrumentation removed afterward (confirmed via `grep -r DEBUG- client/src server/src`, zero matches) and the client rebuilt clean.

**NOT YET DEPLOYED to the live Railway service** — `~/dev-tools/space-shooter` is not its own git repo (it's a subdirectory inside an unrelated, unintentionally-git-tracked home-directory repo pointed at `chibby025/bookbot` — do not commit/push there) and no Railway CLI session is authenticated in this environment. The fix exists only in the local checkout pending the user's deploy (Railway CLI login + `railway up`, or whatever the user's normal deploy path for this service is).

### Stellar Swarm — DEPRECATED, fully removed from the codebase (2026-07-27)
Decided to retire this game (all WeWatch-side integration code) in favor of shifting focus to lightweight multiplayer board games instead. Removed: `frontend/src/components/Games/ShooterGame.jsx` (deleted), `frontend/src/utils/devicePerformance.js` (deleted — confirmed unused by DOOM or anything else), the `space_shooter` entries in `GameLobbyModal.jsx`'s `games` array, `GameOverlay.jsx`'s switch case + lazy import, `GameRulesButton.jsx`'s `RULES` map, and the backend allowlist entries in `game_manager.go` (`validGameTypes`), `websocket_handler.go` (`gamePosterURL`, `minPlayersOverride`). The `roomId` prop on `GameOverlay` and the `allowHeavyGames`/`heavy` filtering mechanism in `GameLobbyModal.jsx` were both kept — shared with `vs_battle` and `doom` respectively.
The separate Railway service (`space-shooter-production-6e0d.up.railway.app`, the `~/dev-tools/space-shooter` local checkout) is **not** managed by this repo and needs to be torn down separately by the user (no Railway CLI access in this dev environment). All the phase history above (bug fixes, architecture writeups) is left as-is per this file's own convention of not editing over past entries.

### Quake 3 / OpenArena — PAUSED mid-port, single-player client inits cleanly, multiplayer never tested (2026-06-27/28)

User asked to plan a true N-player arena shooter via QuakeJS (`begleysm/quakejs` fork, MIT — chosen over `jdarpinian/ioq3` specifically because it already ships a working Node.js WS multiplayer server+client transport, unlike DOOM which needed a hand-built relay from scratch). Approved plan (per-room isolated child process per active WeWatch room, one shared Railway service as supervisor) is saved at `C:\Users\Chinw\.claude\plans\snappy-jumping-giraffe.md`. **Status: Plan Step 1 ("stand up QuakeJS locally, confirm it works") is the only step attempted. Not complete — paused by explicit user request to revisit "some other time," not abandoned due to a dead end.** Local-only work, nothing deployed, nothing committed to a repo WeWatch depends on.

**Where the actual code lives (all outside this repo, untouched by anything WeWatch loads):**
- `~/dev-tools/quake3_fork/` — the QuakeJS fork + `ioq3/` engine submodule (extensively patched, see below) + `bin/web.js`/`bin/content.js` dev servers + `content_assets/baseoa/` (curated OpenArena asset paks).
- `~/dev-tools/oa_gamelogic/` — clone of `OpenArena/gamecode` (GitHub), built to produce real `cgame.qvm`/`qagame.qvm`/`ui.qvm` (see "no real QVMs" gotcha below).
- `/tmp/build_q3.sh`, `/tmp/patch_stackalloc.py`, `/tmp/check_qvms.sh` — throwaway build/patch scripts, gone on next reboot, not committed anywhere. Re-derivable from this section if resuming.

**End result reached**: the client (`ioquake3.js`/`.wasm`) now runs, in a real headless-Chromium browser test, all the way through: asset manifest fetch → mounting all curated `baseoa` paks → renderer init (genuine WebGL context, real GL extensions reported, no GL errors) → sound init (OpenAL/WebAudio) → loading and JIT-compiling a real OpenArena `ui.qvm` (not a stub) → a full `vid_restart` cycle (renderer + UI VM torn down and re-initialized) → **zero JavaScript errors anywhere in this entire pipeline**. The HTML/CSS loading screen (logo + per-pak progress bar) renders and updates correctly throughout. This is a 2014-era codebase against a modern (5.0.2) Emscripten SDK — almost every fix below is "old Emscripten API X needs modern equivalent Y," not a logic bug.

**What was never confirmed**: an actual rendered game frame (map geometry, menu UI, anything visually beyond the HTML loading screen) — headless Chromium+SwiftShader becomes unresponsive (the *compositor*/screenshot pipeline hangs; `page.evaluate()` calls keep working, confirming the JS thread itself is alive) roughly 30-40 seconds in, right after init completes and the main render loop would start. **Multiplayer netcode was never touched at all** — no dedicated-server runtime test, no client→server connection, no two-client test. The dedicated server (`ioq3ded.js`) built and linked successfully but was never run.

**Fixes applied, in the order found (full detail/code in conversation history if resuming — this is the index):**
1. **npm dependency**: removed unpublished `quakejs-files@0.0.3` from `package.json` (only consumer, `bin/repak.js`, isn't needed for this asset-curation approach).
2. **SDL headers for `qgl.h`**: `BASE_CFLAGS += -I$(SDLHDIR)/include` was wired up for every platform except `js` in `ioq3/Makefile` — added it, fixing `GLenum`/`GLuint` unknown-type errors.
3. **Obsolete Emscripten APIs** across `sys_common.js`/`sys_browser.js`/`sys_node.js`/`vm_js.js` (all `--js-library` JS-glue files, not C): `Pointer_stringify`→`UTF8ToString`, `Runtime.stackSave/stackRestore`→bare `stackSave/stackRestore`, `Runtime.generateStructInfo`→removed (replaced with a hand-written `_genStructInfo` helper or deleted as confirmed-dead installer-flow code), `Runtime.dynCall`/`Runtime.addFunction`→bare globals, `makeGetSlabs`/`makeCopyValues` macro helpers→direct `HEAPU8` ops.
4. **Linker flag drift**: removed obsolete `-s OUTLINING_LIMIT`/`-s DLOPEN_SUPPORT`; removed `'_VM_Syscall'` from `EXPORTED_FUNCTIONS` (symbol doesn't exist anywhere in this codebase — stale 2014 leftover).
5. **`-fPIC` missing for SIDE_MODULE builds** (`cgame.js`/`qagame.js`/`ui.js`) — `SHLIBCFLAGS` was never set for `js` platform.
6. **`code/sdl/sdl_input.c`/`sdl_snd.c` don't compile against modern Emscripten's SDL1.2 headers** (old API removed) — excluded for `js` platform, replaced with `code/null/null_input.c` + a new narrow `code/sys/sys_browser_snddma.c` (couldn't reuse `null_snddma.c` as-is — it also redefines `S_RegisterSound`/etc, colliding with the real `client/snd_main.c` symbols this platform's client *does* compile, unlike the dedicated server).
7. **`renderergl1/tr_shade.c`**: `qglArrayElement` (legacy GL 1.1, unimplemented by Emscripten's GL emulation under any name) → swapped to the same file's own existing `R_ArrayElementDiscrete` equivalent.
8. **`renderergl1/tr_surface.c`**: `RB_SurfaceDisplayList` called `qglCallList` — the function's own comment already said "this isn't implemented yet" (SF_DISPLAY_LIST is never actually emitted by real map data) — made it a no-op, matching the adjacent `RB_SurfaceSkip`.
9. **`-lidbfs.js`** needed explicitly (modern Emscripten doesn't auto-link it) for the persistent-FS code path.
10. **`callMain`/`EXPORTED_RUNTIME_METHODS` + module-export semantics**: modern Emscripten (no `MODULARIZE`) expects the *page* to pre-declare `var ioq3 = {}` before the build script loads (`Module = typeof ioq3 != "undefined" ? ioq3 : {}`), and only attaches a *config* object, not necessarily a `Module.callMain` method — ended up calling the bare top-level `callMain` global directly from `index.ejs`, deferred via `Module.onRuntimeInitialized` (NOT `window.onload`, which fires before async WASM instantiation completes — a real race that intermittently dropped the very first `callMain` call).
11. **`emscripten_stack_alloc`/`_restore` aren't real exports for this target** (confirmed: explicitly requesting them via `EXPORTED_FUNCTIONS` fails the *link* with "symbol not found" — they don't exist in any object linked for the client, only the dedicated server happens to pull them in transitively). Worked around with a **post-build JS patch** (`/tmp/patch_stackalloc.py`, regex-based, re-applied after every rebuild) substituting a `malloc`-backed `stackAlloc` and a no-op `stackRestore` — fine since the only caller is a one-time startup `callMain` argv construction, not a hot path.
12. **`addFunction(func, sig)` requires the signature string explicitly in modern Emscripten** — `vm_js.js`'s `addFunction(_VM_Destroy)` (no signature) silently passed `sig=undefined` into `convertJsFunctionToWasm`, which crashes on `sig.slice(1)`. Fixed: `addFunction(_VM_Destroy, 'vi')`.
13. **`dynCall` itself needed adding to `EXPORTED_RUNTIME_METHODS`** (not just renaming away from `Runtime.dynCall` — the bare name also needs to survive dead-code elimination).
14. **`ERRNO_CODES` is a `$`-prefixed shared JS-library dependency in modern Emscripten** (`'$ERRNO_CODES'`) — needs explicit `__deps` declaration on every function referencing it (`Sys_FOpen`-adjacent functions, `SYSC`'s shared deps) or it's silently never linked in (`ReferenceError` at the *use* site, not at declaration).
15. **`Sys_FOpen`'s bare `_fopen(...)` call needs `Sys_FOpen__deps: ['fopen']`** — same "JS calls a bare C global, nothing tells the linker it's needed" pattern as `memset`/`memcpy`/`strncpy` (found later, item 19).
16. **A pre-existing 2014-era bug, not an Emscripten-version issue**: `DirtyInstallers()` takes no `callback` parameter but references one (`return callback(new Error(...))`) on its "asset not found in manifest" branch — never reachable for the commercial Q3 demo's own real installers (which always match), but unconditionally reachable for OpenArena (which never matches the commercial installer names at all). Fixed by emptying the now-dead `installers` array entirely (this project only ever uses free OpenArena data) rather than patching the bug in code nobody needs.
17. **SDL flag-value mismatch — the single most impactful bug found.** `code/SDL12/include/SDL_video.h` (this project's own bundled real-SDL1.2-compatible header) defines `SDL_OPENGL=0x2`, `SDL_HWSURFACE=0x1`, etc — real SDL1.2 values. But the *JS-side* SDL emulation actually used at runtime (Emscripten's own built-in `library_sdl.js`) was generated against **Emscripten's own bundled** `SDL_compat.h`, which uses *completely different* bit values (`SDL_OPENGL=0x4000000`, `SDL_HWSURFACE=0x8000001`, confirmed via `emsdk/.../cache/sysroot/include/SDL/SDL_compat.h`). Every `SDL_SetVideoMode` call's `SDL_OPENGL` flag was silently lost crossing this boundary, so every video-mode request landed on the JS glue's `else` branch (`canvas.getContext("2d")`) instead of WebGL — and a canvas's context type is permanently fixed on first call, so the *real* `getContext("webgl", ...)` call later always returned `null` ("WebGL: TODO" style failures, manifesting as a generic `glGetString`/`getParameter` crash on `undefined`). Fixed with `#ifdef EMSCRIPTEN` in `SDL_video.h`, substituting Emscripten's expected values for this platform only — confirmed via a temporary `HTMLCanvasElement.prototype.getContext` monkey-patch logging every call+stack, which caught the canvas being bound to `'2d'` *before* the real `'webgl'` request ever ran.
18. **`glDrawBuffer` is an unimplemented Emscripten GL-emulation TODO stub** (`abort("glDrawBuffer: TODO")` — calls `abort()` unconditionally) — patched to a no-op via the same post-build script (a WebGL default framebuffer only has one effective draw target regardless of GL_FRONT/GL_BACK).
19. **`re.UpdateMode` is only ever assigned by `renderergl2`** (`tr_init.c`); this build uses `renderergl1` (chosen specifically to avoid `renderergl2`'s custom-GLSL shader-compatibility risk) — `renderergl1` never sets it, leaving a null function pointer that the `#if EMSCRIPTEN` "fast vid_restart" hack in `cl_main.c` called unconditionally on the very first `vid_restart`, crashing with a genuine WASM `call_indirect` "null function" trap. Found by relinking with `-g2` specifically to get real C function names in the trace (`CL_Vid_Restart_f` ← `Cmd_ExecuteString` ← `Cbuf_Execute` ← `Sys_Frame`) instead of bare `wasm-function[N]` indices. Fixed with `if (!strcmp(arg, "fast") && re.UpdateMode)` — falls through to the normal/slow vid_restart path, which doesn't need it.
20. **`memset`/`memcpy`/`strncpy` referenced as bare globals inside `vm_js.js`'s QVM-syscall-fast-path code-generator strings** — invisible to the compiler's own dependency analysis since they're inside string literals being `eval`'d at runtime, not real calls. Added to `VM_Compile__deps`.
21. **No real OpenArena QVM bytecode exists in *any* Debian/Ubuntu apt package** (`openarena-085-data`, `-088-data`, `-server`, the base `openarena-data` — checked all of them) — every one ships either a 20-29 byte placeholder stub (literal text starting `NTVE...`, not real bytecode) or a native `x86_64.so` (useless for a browser/WASM target; the packages are built assuming a real desktop install always prefers native code over portable QVM). Fixed by cloning `OpenArena/gamecode` from GitHub and building it directly with `make` — it bundles its own complete copy of the `q3lcc`/`q3asm` toolchain source and just works, producing real, correctly-magic-numbered (`VM_MAGIC_VER2`) multi-hundred-KB QVMs.
22. **The engine's own pak manifest filter silently requires filenames to literally start with `pak`** (`isCommon()`'s regex in `sys_common.js`: `(com_basegame|fs_game)/pak.+\.pk3$`) — my own `z_`-prefixed override paks (the gamelogic QVM pak, and a pre-existing OpenArena community map pack `z_oacmp-volume1-v3.pk3` that — newly discovered — had *also* silently never loaded, since the session began, for the same reason) were completely excluded from the engine's search path, with no error, no log line, nothing. Fixed by renaming both to `pak8-`/`pak9-` prefixes (high numbers, so they still win priority over the lower-numbered stock paks they're meant to override).
23. **Missing `sound/misc/silence.wav`** — not present in any OpenArena package at all (appears to be a Quake-3-engine-level hardcoded default, not OpenArena content); synthesized a tiny silent WAV via `ffmpeg` and packaged it into the pak set, same pattern as the earlier synthesized `default.cfg`.

**Open investigation, not resolved**: tried the same `SAFE_HEAP=1`/`ASSERTIONS=2` methodology that found 2 real memory-safety bugs in the DOOM WASM port, hoping to turn the headless-tab-hang into a catchable error. Got blocked by an unrelated, pre-existing issue first: dozens of call sites across `sys_common.js`/`sys_browser.js`/`sys_node.js` use the legacy 3-argument `allocate(slab, 'i8', ALLOC_STACK)` form (silently tolerated without `ASSERTIONS` — the extra arg just gets dropped, defaulting to a slightly-wrong-but-harmless heap allocation instead of stack); `ASSERTIONS` of any level >0 hard-aborts on this with `"allocate no longer takes a type argument"`, well before reaching the actual code path under investigation. Fixing all of those call sites to properly use `SAFE_HEAP` was judged not worth it for a one-off diagnostic — reverted cleanly back to the same flags that produced the known-good zero-JS-error build. **If resuming: the fastest next step is testing in a real, visible, non-headless browser** (not available in this dev environment) to determine whether the hang is a genuine bug or purely a headless+SwiftShader compositor artifact — this is the same open question the DOOM project's own "known, unresolved issue" section flags for its superficially similar symptom.

**If resuming this work**, the actual next steps in priority order: (1) confirm a real frame renders in a real browser, (2) run `ioq3ded.js` and confirm it actually starts a server (never done), (3) point a client at it and confirm a connection (never done), (4) only then proceed to Plan Steps 2-6 (N-siblings process-isolation test, the supervisor, Railway deploy, WeWatch frontend/backend wiring) — none of which have been started.

### Lightweight deathmatch / multiplayer game candidates — survey for the lighter alternative track (2026-06-28)

Following the Quake3 pause, revisited the original candidate shortlist from earlier in this project's "what else could we add" discussions, now with the benefit of two completed heavy-3D integrations (DOOM, Stellar Swarm) and one paused one (Quake3) to compare against:

| Candidate | Status this session | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| **AssaultCube** | Previously surveyed (pre-Quake3) | **Real legal blocker** — asset license is non-commercial-only freeware. Disqualified regardless of technical fit. |
| **Frets on Fire X** | Previously surveyed | Python/Pygame, no mature WASM compilation path, zero multiplayer support at all (it's a single-player rhythm game). Disqualified — doesn't fit the "deathmatch/multiplayer" goal at all. |
| **Quake 3 / OpenArena** | This session, paused | Technically real and clean (GPL engine + GPL-clean OpenArena assets, no Freedoom-style gray area), but a 2014-era WASM port against a modern toolchain costs many hours of small compatibility fixes (23 found and fixed this session alone) before even reaching a confirmed-working multiplayer test. Highest ceiling (real id-Tech-3 arena shooter, real existing Node.js multiplayer server), highest cost paid so far, cost remaining unknown. |
| **FreeKick3** | Previously surveyed | Abandoned since 2015, zero networking of any kind. Disqualified — would need multiplayer built from scratch on top of an unmaintained base, the worst of both worlds. |
| **LudoKing** | New to this round | Not actually a deathmatch/multiplayer-shooter candidate — Ludo is a board game (already shipped this session as WeWatch's own hand-built `Ludo`, see above — "Ludo King" was referenced there only as the *house-rule variant name*, not a codebase to integrate). Likely a mix-up with the existing Ludo feature rather than a distinct new candidate; flagged for the user to clarify if a different game was actually meant.

**Not yet surveyed, worth a real look next time this track comes up** (none investigated this session — pure carry-over from the "New games" candidate list documented earlier in this file): `OpenArena`-adjacent but lighter native ports aside, the **non-shooter variety candidates already on file** (`pmndrs/racing-game` MIT react-three-fiber, `micro-racing`, `NotBlox`) and the **niche light-3D pick** `nickyvanurk/3d-multiplayer-browser-shooter` remain the most promising *un-started* options specifically because Stellar Swarm (built from a sibling/cousin of that last repo) already proved the "small three.js + plain WebSocket + server-authoritative" pattern works well and integrates cleanly with WeWatch's existing per-room model — a second game built the same way is a known-cost bet, unlike Quake3's still-partially-unknown remaining cost.

### Posts (updated 2026-05)
- `Post` model has **no `Title` field** — removed from Go struct (DB column still exists but is ignored by GORM; optionally drop with `ALTER TABLE posts DROP COLUMN IF EXISTS title`).
- Use `description` as the display text everywhere title was shown (truncate to ~60–80 chars where needed). Do not add a title field back.
- `PostType` values: `"recording"` | `"upload"` | `"text"`. `MediaType` values: `"video"` | `"image"` | `"gif"`.
- **Text posts** (`post_type = "text"`): store raw text in `text_content TEXT` column (added 2026-05); no file upload, no canvas thumbnail. Frontend detects `post.post_type === 'text'` and renders a dark text card instead of media.
- `CreatePostRequest` and `UpdatePostRequest` have no `Title` field. Search in `GetDiscoverFeed` covers `description ILIKE` + `username ILIKE` only (title clause removed).
- Notification body for new room posts uses `post.Description[:60]` preview.
- `generateDownloadFilename` in `post_download_handler.go` uses description preview instead of title.
- **DB migration needed** for text posts (add `text_content` column — see DB section below).

### LobbyPage Feed Structure (updated 2026-05)
- **Two sub-tabs only**: "Watching Live" and "Feed". No Following tab. `useFeedAlgorithm` is stripped to For You only (following feed code removed).
- **Swipe**: bidirectional between Watching Live ↔ Feed only. No third level.
- **FABs**:
  - Watching Live sub-tab: W icon (`/icons/lwoIcon.png`) FAB → opens `CreateNewModal` with `hidePosts={true}` (Watch Now + Watch in Rooms only, no Post option).
  - Feed sub-tab: Plus icon FAB → opens `PostUploadModal` directly.
  - Rooms tab: Plus icon FAB → opens `CreateNewModal` with `hidePosts={false}` (all options).
- **CreateNewModal** has `hidePosts = false` prop. When `true`, the Post button is hidden.
- **Feed search**: always-visible search bar at top of Feed content (not collapsible), same style as sessions search bar.
- **Post dividers**: `divide-y divide-gray-200 dark:divide-gray-700` on the posts list (not `space-y`).
- **Tab font sizes**: main tabs (`text-sm sm:text-base`), sub-tabs (`text-base font-semibold`).
- **Room ordering**: `sortRooms()` pins owned rooms first, then sorts member rooms by content-rating proximity to the user's own room rating using `RATING_ORDER` index distance.
- **Room cards**: no "Your Room" badge text — purple ring border only (`ring-2 ring-purple-500`).

### Content Rating
- Ratings: G, PG, Educational, Religious, 13+, 16+, 18+, Mature
- `User.CanViewContent(rating)` enforces age gate based on `User.DateOfBirth`
- Mature rating = adult content firewall, NOT a porn hosting feature. App store hard wall: Capacitor (Phase 3) build variant will disable Mature content for iOS/Android App Store compliance.
- `UserPreferences` model already handles client-side content filtering

### Church Mode / LiveShare Studio Controls
