- `VideoWatch.jsx`: `device_stream_ready`'s handler computes `isOwnFreshUpload` (host + matching `uploader_id` + matching `upload_id` + the File object still present) and branches — local path builds `currentMedia` with `mediaUrl` deliberately omitted (set later) and `remoteMediaUrl: readyMediaUrl` stashed as the error-fallback target; new `localPlaybackFile` state + a dedicated `useEffect` owns the blob URL's whole lifecycle (`URL.createObjectURL`/`URL.revokeObjectURL`) — deliberately state-driven rather than a ref mutated inline in the WS handler, since a ref-based cleanup could race and revoke a blob that had already been replaced a moment earlier (the effect's cleanup closes over its own local `url` const, immune to that race). Cleared (triggering revocation) at every natural "moving on" point: switching media, deleting the item, video ending, decode failure.
- `useStreamBufferHealth.js`: all three gated effects (Tier 2 one-shot, Tier 1 continuous monitoring) now also check `!currentMedia.isLocalPlayback` — local playback has no network dependency at all, nothing to monitor.
- `CinemaVideoPlayer.jsx`: the plain-URL branch's native `<video>` `error` listener now forwards to `onErrorRef` **only** when `mediaItem?.isLocalPlayback` — other media types on this branch (plain remote URLs, Drive-uploaded links) keep their original log-only behavior, not touched.
- `handleError` (`VideoWatch.jsx`): new branch — if `currentMedia.isLocalPlayback && currentMedia.remoteMediaUrl`, swap `mediaUrl` to the remote URL, clear `isLocalPlayback`, and show a toast ("Playing from server — this file needs the server's version to play here") rather than erroring out. Covers the case where the raw local file needs actual server-side transcoding to decode (e.g. some MKV/AVI sources) that the host's own browser can't do natively.
- Diagnostic logging added, tagged `[LocalPlayback]`: one line per `device_stream_ready` (role + which path + URL), a diagnostic-only line when a host unexpectedly lands on the remote path (breaks down which precondition failed), and lines on blob create/revoke/fallback.

**Confirmed environment-agnostic**: `blob:` URLs are a pure browser API, always same-origin, never subject to CORS; checked `frontend/vercel.json` and confirmed no CSP header restricts `media-src` on Vercel. Should behave identically on localhost and Vercel.

**Known, accepted limitation**: a host refresh mid-stream loses the in-tab `File` reference (browsers don't persist file handles across reloads) — falls back to the same remote HLS URL everyone else uses, same as before this feature existed. Explicitly scoped out of v1 (Cache Storage duplication and File System Access API were both discussed as possible v2 approaches, each with real tradeoffs — not built).

**NOT LIVE-TESTED — this is exactly the kind of change this codebase's device-streaming history has repeatedly found real bugs in only through live two-browser testing.** Test plan: host streams a file, opens Network tab, confirms no re-download of the HLS manifest/segments while playing; a second "member" browser confirms it still receives the normal HLS stream; force a decode failure (or test with a source needing actual transcoding) and confirm the toast + fallback to remote plays correctly; confirm deleting the item while playing revokes the blob (check DevTools memory/no lingering blob: URL) and stops playback for both browsers.

### Stream from Device — 6-part storage/orphan cleanup fix (added 2026-08-05)
User reported "Failed to upload chunk 83 after 3 attempts: Request failed with status code 500" appearing when ending a session mid-upload, and — separately — the *same stale error* reappearing later on an unrelated, brand-new session. Also reported (after logging into the BunnyCDN dashboard directly) that session previews were never actually being wiped when sessions ended.

**Root causes, all confirmed by reading the actual code, not guessed:**
1. `handleLeaveRoom` never cancelled the in-flight chunk-upload loop. Since that loop is a plain async JS chain (not tied to React lifecycle), unmounting `VideoWatch.jsx` doesn't stop it — it kept retrying against a session the backend had already torn down, eventually threw, and `toast.error(...)` (a global react-hot-toast call, not scoped to any component) rendered on whatever page — including a brand new session — the user had since navigated to.
2. `uploadAbortControllerRef.current.abort()` was called by `handleCancelUpload` but the signal was never actually threaded into `uploadChunksParallel`/`uploadChunkWithRetry`/`uploadChunk`'s axios call — cancellation only ever reset local UI state, never stopped real network requests.
3. `EndWatchSessionHandler`'s cleanup goroutine calls `AbortProgressiveUploadForSession`, which (pre-progressive-mode) does `os.RemoveAll(uploadDir)` immediately — but chunks upload 2-6 at a time concurrently, and nothing serialized that deletion against in-flight writes for the *same* upload. Whichever chunk's `SaveUploadedFile`/`os.Rename` straddled the deletion got a genuine OS-level failure → 500 (explains "chunk 83" — an arbitrary number, whichever chunk lost the race).
4. `ChunkUploadHandler` had zero "is this session still alive" check — a chunk arriving after (or racing) the abort got silently written to disk and could resurrect an already-removed `ProgressiveUploadState` via `GetOrCreateProgressiveUpload`, re-linking it to the dead session — the exact risk `AbortProgressiveUpload`'s own doc comment already named but never actually prevented.
5. **`DeleteMediaFile`'s `.m3u8` branch only ever removed the local manifest directory.** Once a segment is uploaded to BunnyCDN, its local copy is deleted (by design) — meaning by the time *any* cleanup path ran, there was nothing left locally to remove for segments already on the CDN, and nothing anywhere in the codebase deleted the `device_streams/{uploadID}/` CDN folder. Every device-stream session left its segments on BunnyCDN storage forever.
6. **`CleanupStaleSessions`** (the `websocket.go` hourly goroutine handling heartbeat-timeout session ends — almost certainly the *majority* of real session ends, since most users close a tab rather than click "End Session") **never called the session-preview cleanup mechanism at all.** `PreviewQueue.ClearSessionPreview` genuinely deletes previews from BunnyCDN correctly (confirmed `BUNNY_PULL_ZONE_URL` matches its `b-cdn.net` string check) and *is* correctly wired into both `EndWatchSessionHandler`'s explicit-end path and `AutoEndSession`'s host-timeout path — but `CleanupStaleSessions` duplicated some cleanup (LiveShare assets, a `TemporaryMediaItem` safety net) without ever including this one. This is the actual, confirmed reason session previews were accumulating on BunnyCDN.

**Fixes, all implemented and build/lint-verified (not live-tested):**
- **Fix 6** (`websocket.go`, `CleanupStaleSessions`): now calls `mediaSwitchHandler.HandleSessionEnd(session.SessionID)` + `CleanupSessionPreviews(session.SessionID)` per session in its loop, mirroring `EndWatchSessionHandler`/`AutoEndSession`.
- **Fix 5** (`chunk_upload.go`, `ChunkUploadHandler`): new early check — if `session_id` resolves to a `WatchSession` with `ended_at` already set, reject the chunk with `410 Gone` (`{"code": "session_ended"}`) before anything touches disk.
- **Fix 2** (`temporary_media_items.go`, `DeleteSingleTemporaryMediaItemHandler`): now calls `utils.AbortProgressiveUploadForSession(item.SessionID)` before deleting a stream item's files, if `item.IsStream` — matches the exact mechanism session-end already uses. Known, accepted minor imprecision (same as the existing session-end call): matches by session ID, not upload ID, so deleting an old *completed* item while a *different* new upload is genuinely still in flight for the same session would incorrectly abort that new upload too — judged rare enough in practice not to warrant a bigger schema change (would need a new `UploadID` field to disambiguate precisely).
- **Fix 1** (biggest piece): new `CDNSegmentsPrefix string` field on `TemporaryMediaItem` (auto-migrated — already in `main.go`'s `AutoMigrate` list, no manual SQL needed), populated at both upload-pipeline creation points (`onProgressiveStreamReady` in `chunk_upload.go`, and the fallback path's `newTempMediaItem` construction) with e.g. `device_streams/{uploadID}/` whenever CDN mode was configured for that upload — recorded unconditionally (not gated on per-segment upload success) since a delete-time call against a prefix with nothing there is a harmless no-op, and the prefix is the same for every segment of one upload regardless of how many had individually confirmed uploading at record time. `ProgressiveReadyInfo` gained a matching `CDNRemotePrefix` field to carry this through the existing cross-package callback. New shared helper `deleteTemporaryMediaItemFiles(item)` in `temporary_media_items.go` (file + poster +, if `CDNSegmentsPrefix != ""`, `utils.DeletePathFromBunnyCDNStorage(item.CDNSegmentsPrefix)`) — turned out no new BunnyCDN API code was needed at all, since `DeletePathFromBunnyCDNStorage`'s own doc comment already says "deletes a file **or prefix**", and passing a trailing-slash path was already an established pattern elsewhere (`demo_admin_handler.go`). Wired into **9 call sites**: `DeleteSingleTemporaryMediaItemHandler`, `DeleteTemporaryMediaItemsForRoomHandler`, `EndWatchSessionHandler`'s cleanup goroutine, `AutoEndSession`, the unexported `cleanupSession` (temporary-room path), `CleanupAllTemporaryMedia` (the `CleanupStaleSessions` safety net), `CleanupExpiredSessions`, `CleanupOrphanedInstantWatchRooms`, room deletion's background goroutine, and the WS `leave_session` "last user left" cleanup. Deliberately left `models.MediaItem` (a different, permanent-media model with no `CDNSegmentsPrefix` field) untouched at the two sites that handle it alongside `TemporaryMediaItem`.
- **Fix 4** (`uploadChunker.js`, `useMediaUploadManager.js`): `abortSignal` now threaded through `uploadChunksParallel` → `uploadChunkWithRetry` → `uploadChunk` (which already accepted and used it, just never received it) — `abortController.signal` is now actually passed in `uploadFileChunked`'s call. Also: a `410` response now stops retrying immediately (alongside the existing `CanceledError` short-circuit) rather than burning all 3 attempts against a confirmed-dead session.
- **Fix 3** (`VideoWatch.jsx`): `handleLeaveRoom` now calls `mediaUploadManager.handleCancelUpload()` first thing, before anything else — covers "I clicked leave/end". The `session_ended` WS case handler also calls it — covers the case where the session ends for a reason *other* than this client's own action (host-timeout `AutoEndSession`, or `CleanupStaleSessions`'s sweep) while this client is mid-upload and never called `handleLeaveRoom` at all. `uploadFileChunked`'s catch block now treats a `410` quietly (console log only) instead of `toast.error` — this is what used to surface as the stale "Failed to upload chunk N after 3 attempts" toast on an unrelated later session.

**Verified**: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./internal/utils/...` all clean (only a pre-existing, unrelated vet warning in a different package, and the pre-existing `cmd/` "multiple mains" issue neither of which are from this work); eslint identical problem counts pre/post on every touched file; production `vite build` succeeds.

**NOT LIVE-TESTED — none of this has been exercised against a real running app.** Test plan:
1. Start a device-stream upload, click "End Session" mid-upload — confirm no 500s in the backend log for subsequent chunks, confirm no stale error toast appears on any later page/session.
2. Start a device-stream upload, close the tab (don't click anything) to trigger the heartbeat-timeout path — wait for `CleanupStaleSessions`/`AutoEndSession` to fire (or trigger manually) — confirm the same.
3. Delete a media item while its upload is still actively running — confirm the drainer stops (no further segment-upload log lines for that upload ID after the delete).
4. Check the BunnyCDN dashboard directly after a normal session end (both explicit "End Session" and a timeout-based end) — confirm `device_streams/{uploadID}/` segments and session preview poster/clip are both actually gone, not just the local manifest.
5. Confirm a *legitimate*, still-uploading session is never incorrectly rejected by the new 410 check (i.e. the `ended_at IS NULL` condition is read correctly and doesn't false-positive on an active session).

### Challenge Friends — root cause of "failed to send challenge" diagnosed, fuller redesign proposed, not yet built (discussed 2026-08-04)
User reported the post-game "⚔️ Challenge" flow (`VideoWatch.jsx`'s Challenge Friends pill + modal, `SendGameChallengeHandler` in `watch_out_handler.go`) fails with a generic "Failed to send challenge" toast. **Root cause, confirmed**: `SendGameChallengeHandler` hard-requires an accepted friendship (same policy `SendWatchOutHandler` already enforces platform-wide, for DM anti-spam reasons) — but the modal populates its picker from `roomMembers` (everyone currently in the room), not friends. Challenging a fellow room member you're not already friends with → 403 → the generic catch-all shows an unhelpful toast.

Also found the actual "Accept Challenge" flow is much shallower than it should be: `LobbyMessageBubble.jsx`'s Accept button just does `navigate('/rooms/:room_id')` — no acceptance signal, no host awareness, no game preselection.

**Proposed redesign (discussed, confirmed with user, NOT YET BUILT):**
- Split the picker into two sections: **Friends** (existing DM-card mechanism, friendship-gated, unchanged — works even for someone not currently in the room) and **In This Session** (new — no friendship check needed since they're already co-present, same trust boundary as room chat; a private live WS prompt only to the target, nothing posted to room chat per user's explicit preference).
- Acceptance broadcasts into the room. If the host is one of the two challengers → `GameLobbyModal` opens automatically, preselected to that game (new `initialGameId`/`initialSelectedPlayerIds` props needed), both challengers pre-checked as players. If the host is neither challenger → an animated clash banner (avatars colliding, spark burst, sound) auto-dismisses after a few seconds but leaves a small retrievable badge on the Games button (per user's explicit preference — not just "gone if missed") so the host can reopen the same preselected setup later.
- Host authority is preserved throughout — a challenge is always a suggestion, never a bypass of "only host starts games."

This directly fixes the reported bug too: session-members would route through the new no-friendship-required path instead of the friendship-gated one they were never going to pass.

### Ping Pong — mobile-lag netcode fix + haptic vibration (2026-08-04/05)
User reported real cross-device testing feedback: host (laptop, P1/physics-authority) said the mobile member (P2) "doesn't move fast enough"; the mobile member said they couldn't move fast enough and the ball "passes through their paddle." Root cause: P1's own paddle read is zero-latency (same-process, every RAF frame), but P1's *collision check* against P2's paddle only had P2's last relayed `state_sync` snapshot — throttled to one send per 50ms, plus real network latency on top. A fast-moving mobile player's true position at collision time could be meaningfully ahead of what P1's physics loop was still checking against, so the ball would visually "pass through" a paddle that had actually already moved to block it.

**5 fixes implemented, all approved together:**
1. **Client-side dead-reckoning extrapolation** — `estimateSendVelocity(track, currentX, now)` computes a secant-based velocity estimate from the last two positions sent; `extrapolatePaddleX(anchor, now)` projects the remote paddle's position forward from its last known `{x, vx, t}` anchor, capped at `REMOTE_PADDLE_EXTRAPOLATION_CAP_S = 0.35s` (beyond that, a stale connection stops trying to guess and just holds position). Both P1's collision check and the `draw()` render loop use the extrapolated position for whichever paddle isn't "mine," not the raw last-received snapshot.
2. **Staleness-scaled hit-tolerance forgiveness** — `BASE_HIT_TOLERANCE = 1.20` (flat, both sides) plus `MAX_EXTRA_HIT_FORGIVENESS = 0.25` added to P2's side only, scaled by how much of the 0.35s extrapolation cap has elapsed since the last real P2 update. A perfectly fresh connection gets zero extra forgiveness; a connection that's gone stale for the full cap window gets the maximum — a lightweight, self-contained lag-compensation technique that needed no new wire protocol (no ping/pong RTT measurement was built).
3. **Faster paddle send rate** — `PADDLE_SEND_MS` 50ms → 30ms, reducing the raw staleness window before extrapolation even has to compensate.
4. **Faster on-screen movement speed** — `PADDLE_BTN_SPEED` (mobile ◀▶ button-driven movement) 340 → 480, directly addressing "can't move fast enough." Directional arrow buttons were already present on the mobile paddle (`isTouchDevice`-gated) — confirmed, not newly added.
5. **Haptic vibration** — new `hapticImpact(pattern)` (feature-detected `navigator.vibrate`, independent of the sound-mute toggle). Wired into: paddle hit (short pulse) and smash hit (stronger pattern) — P1 gets a direct trigger at the exact physics-loop collision moment, P2 infers the same events from consecutive relayed `state_sync` deltas (a paddle hit always flips `vy`'s sign in this game's physics, since paddles are strictly top/bottom); goal; pickup grab; and effect-activation (freeze/slow/Ghost Ball), gated so only the actual effect *target* feels it, not both players.

**Backend** (`ping_pong.go`): `"state_sync"` case now also captures `p1vx` (P1's self-reported velocity) into `GameData`; `"paddle_move"` case now also captures `p2vx`. Both are pure pass-through relay fields with zero server-side validation — same trust model as every other ball/paddle field in this game — and flow automatically through `pingPongPublicState` (already returns the whole map, no per-field allowlist to update).

**Verified**: `go build`/`go vet` clean on the games package; `eslint` on `PingPongGame.jsx` clean; full-project eslint problem count unchanged (981, confirmed via `git stash` before/after comparison) — no regressions introduced elsewhere. **NOT LIVE-TESTED** — no live browser/multi-device session was run. Test plan: host (laptop) vs. a real mobile device, confirm the ball no longer visibly passes through a fast-moving mobile paddle; confirm mobile paddle now feels responsive to on-screen ◀▶ taps; confirm vibration fires on a real phone for paddle hits, smashes, goals, and pickup grabs (and does *not* fire for the non-target player's effect-activation pulses).

### Air Hockey — ported Ping Pong's mobile-lag netcode fix + haptic vibration (2026-08-05)
Same root cause and same 5-part fix as the Ping Pong section above, ported to Air Hockey's 2-D mallet physics (Ping Pong's paddles move on one axis only; Air Hockey's mallets move freely in a 2-D plane, so every helper is a 2-D variant rather than a reused 1-D one).

**`AirHockeyGame.jsx`**: `MALLET_SEND_MS` 40ms → 25ms; new `MAX_EXTRA_MALLET_FORGIVENESS = 8` (px, staleness-scaled) and `REMOTE_MALLET_EXTRAPOLATION_CAP_S = 0.35`; `estimateSendVelocity2D(track, x, y, now)` / `extrapolateMalletPos(anchor, now, yMin, yMax)` mirror the Ping Pong helpers but operate on `{x, y}` pairs and clamp the extrapolated Y to the mallet's own legal half of the table. `S.current` gained `remoteMalletAnchor`/`p1SendTrack`/`p2SendTrack`. `checkMallet(mx, my, extraRadius = 0)` widens the effective collision radius for P1's check against P2's (extrapolated) mallet, scaled by staleness the same way Ping Pong scales hit-tolerance; P1's own mallet check is unaffected (0 extra radius, since it's always fresh). `draw()` uses the extrapolated position for whichever mallet isn't "mine," for all three roles (P1/P2/spectator). Both `state_sync` sends (playing-phase and serving-phase) now include `p1vx`/`p1vy`; the `mallet_move` send (P2's pointer-move handler) now includes `p2vx`/`p2vy` — both via `estimateSendVelocity2D`.

**Vibration**: `hapticImpact(pattern)` wired into goal (`[20,40,20]`, gated `s.isP2` so only P2's device buzzes for the inferred side — P1 gets its own direct trigger) and mallet hit (inferred via **speed increase**, not a sign-flip like Ping Pong — Air Hockey's arbitrary-angle mallet reflections don't cleanly flip one velocity axis, but a mallet hit is the only event that increases puck speed via `SPEED_UP`, so comparing consecutive relayed speeds against `MALLET_HIT_SPEED_RATIO` reliably distinguishes a hit from a plain wall bounce).

**Backend** (`air_hockey.go`): `"state_sync"` case now also captures `p1vx`/`p1vy`; `"mallet_move"` case now also captures `p2vx`/`p2vy` — same pure pass-through relay pattern as Ping Pong, flowing through `airHockeyPublicState` (already whole-map, no allowlist).

**Verified**: `go build`/`go vet` clean; `eslint` on `AirHockeyGame.jsx` clean; full-project eslint problem count unchanged (981, confirmed via `git stash` before/after); full production `vite build` succeeds. **NOT LIVE-TESTED**. Test plan: same as Ping Pong's above, adapted for 2-D movement — confirm a fast-moving mobile mallet no longer lets the puck pass through it; confirm vibration fires on goal and mallet hits on a real phone.

### Snakes & Ladders — warp pads, trap squares, board color contrast pass (added 2026-08-05)
User asked for better board coloring and more variety than plain snakes/ladders. Discussed several options (warp pads, trap squares, boost squares, swap squares, shield squares); user picked warp + trap specifically.

**New mechanics** (`snakes_ladders.go`):
- `warpPads map[int]int` (`11→44`, `46→16`, `79→97`) — a third teleport type, same non-recursive single-lookup model as `snakeLadders`, but direction isn't implied by the square (unlike ladder=up/snake=down) — you don't know if it's good until you land. All three new maps (`warpPads`, `trapSquares`) are disjoint from `snakeLadders` and from each other by construction (verified by hand before picking squares), so lookup order between them doesn't matter.
- `trapSquares map[int]bool` (`30`, `58`, `68`, `90`) — landing here doesn't move you, but sets a `skip_next` flag (new `[]bool` in `GameData`, same lazy-init + JSON-roundtrip-coercion getter pattern as `positions`) that costs this player their *next* turn.
- **Trap consumption reuses the existing "roll" move type rather than adding a new one**: `processSnakesLaddersMove` checks `skipNext[playerIdx]` at the very top — if set, it clears the flag, tags `last_event: "skipped"`, and returns with no dice roll at all (generic turn-advance still moves play on). The client's own "Roll Dice" button (relabeled "😵 Trapped — Tap to Continue" when trapped) sends the exact same `{move_type: "roll"}` it always did — no new wire message needed.
- **A real ordering bug caught before it shipped, not after**: originally, landing on a trap unconditionally set `skip_next`, including when the same roll was also a 6 (which grants an immediate extra turn on the *same* `CurrentTurn`). Since the extra-turn mechanism doesn't advance `CurrentTurn`, the very next move by that same player (their bonus roll) would hit the function again with the flag already set — and get immediately, wrongly consumed as a skip, silently cancelling the bonus roll they'd just earned. Fixed by making a 6 dodge the trap entirely (`eventType = "trap_dodged"`, flag never set) — reads as a nice bonus interaction ("🍀 LUCKY SIX! rolled right out of the trap") rather than an awkward edge-case patch, and eliminates the ordering conflict at the source instead of trying to detect/suppress it after the fact.
- Every move now tags an explicit `last_event` (`ladder`/`snake`/`warp`/`trap`/`trap_dodged`/`overshoot`/`skipped`/`""`) in `GameData`, broadcast unchanged like every other field (this game was already perfect-information, no `publicGameData` filtering needed).

**Frontend** (`SnakesAndLaddersGame.jsx`):
- Mirrors `WARP_PADS`/`TRAP_SQUARES` for board rendering only (icons, tints, tooltips) — same "never for move validation" convention the file's own header comment already stated for `SNAKES_LADDERS`, now extended to also cover event *classification*: the event-banner logic was rewritten to trust the server's `last_event` tag directly instead of re-deriving it from position deltas, since a warp pad's direction can't be inferred the way ladder-up/snake-down could.
- New `WarpIcon` (a two-tone spiral, purple→cyan, deliberately non-directional) and `TrapIcon` (an orange bear-trap/jaws shape) SVGs, matching the existing hand-drawn `LadderIcon`/`SnakeIcon` style.
- `skip_next` synced into new `skipNext` state; a small 🕸️ badge appears on a trapped player's pill (visible to everyone, not just them) for the duration of the flag; the roll button switches to a red/orange "Trapped" style and copy when `skipNext[currentTurn]` is true for the current player.
- The `'skipped'` event is handled as its own early-return branch in the sync effect, before the roll-based slide/climb animation math — a skip has no dice roll and no position change, so folding it into the existing roll-diff logic would have derived nonsense from a stale `last_roll` value.

**Board color contrast pass** (same file, addressing the original "better coloring" ask directly): the checkerboard's two alternating base shades were ~15 lightness points apart (`#2d3548`/`#242b3d` vs `#242b3d`/`#1c2333`) — close enough to read as nearly flat. Widened the gap (`#3d4560`/`#323a52` vs `#232a3d`/`#1a2030`) so the grid pattern itself is legible at a glance. Ladder/snake cell tints bumped from ~60% opacity (`99`/`88` hex alpha) to ~80% (`cc`) so "danger/opportunity" squares read from across the board, not just via hover tooltip — warp and trap use the same `cc` opacity for consistency. Square numbers bumped 8px→9px and 35%→45% opacity for legibility.

**Verified**: `go build`/`go vet` clean on the games package; `eslint` clean on both touched files (`SnakesAndLaddersGame.jsx`, `GameRulesButton.jsx`); full-project eslint count unchanged (981); full production `vite build` succeeds. **NOT LIVE-TESTED** — no live browser session was run. Test plan: play a full game with 2+ real accounts; land on a warp pad and confirm the teleport direction matches what the backend computed (check both a forward and backward warp); land on a trap without rolling a 6 and confirm the trapped player's next "Roll Dice" click correctly skips with no dice change and the 🕸️ badge disappears afterward; land on a trap while rolling a 6 and confirm the "LUCKY SIX" banner shows instead and the player is NOT flagged trapped; confirm the board's contrast/legibility improvement reads correctly on a real phone screen, not just a desktop monitor.

### RoomTV — one-time "Join" coach-mark; Taskbar initial-visible window shortened to 30s (added 2026-08-05)
Two small, unrelated follow-ups.

**RoomTV Join tour** (`RoomTV.jsx`): new one-time `Coachmark` (the same reusable spotlight-tour component already used by Taskbar/LeftSidebar/RoomPageNew/SettingsModal) pointing at the "Join" button in the live-session strip. Fires the first time a room page's RoomTV content becomes `type === 'session'` — covers both the "landed on a room page that already has a live session" case and the "session starts while I'm already on the room page" case, since both are really the same underlying event (RoomTV's `content` transitioning into the session type) and the ref-guard (`joinTourTriggeredRef`) plus `localStorage` key (`wewatch_roomtv_join_tour_seen`) make either path safely one-time regardless of which one actually fires it. A 700ms delay before showing lets the strip's own fade/expand animation settle first, matching the pattern other tours in this codebase already use. Registered in `SettingsModal.jsx`'s `TOURS` replay list (Help tab) alongside the existing Room/Settings/Sidebar/Taskbar tours — falls through to the existing generic replay branch (`localStorage.removeItem` + a toast hint) with no special-casing needed, since RoomTV has no dedicated "jump to a room with a live session" navigation the way the Room Page tour's replay button has for `/rooms/:id`.

**Taskbar initial-visible window**: `INITIAL_VISIBLE_MS` 60000 → 30000. This is the "how long does the pill stay up on first mount before the normal short idle-hide timers take over" constant — a fresh session join and a page refresh both remount `Taskbar`, so both cases were already covered by the same `useEffect([])` with no extra wiring needed; only the duration itself needed to change.

**Verified**: `eslint` clean on all three touched files (`RoomTV.jsx`, `Taskbar.jsx`, `SettingsModal.jsx` — the pre-existing unused-prop lint errors reported on `Taskbar.jsx`/`RoomTV.jsx` are unrelated to this change, confirmed none reference any of the new code); full-project eslint count unchanged (981); full production `vite build` succeeds. **NOT LIVE-TESTED**. Test plan: join (or refresh into) a room page that has a live session for the first time ever on a given browser/account and confirm the Join coach-mark appears once, pointing at the real button, and never reappears on a later visit; confirm it also fires if a session starts while already sitting on the room page; confirm the Taskbar pill now hides ~30s after a fresh join/refresh (down from ~60s) if left untouched; confirm Settings → Help → Tours → "RoomTV Join Tour" replay correctly re-arms it.

### Wordsmith — rack now always visible; clearer exchange confirm step (added 2026-08-05)
User reported the Exchange button "does nothing," and asked whether exchange was meant to be a solo action or a 2-player trade/popup. Separately reported not being able to see refilled tiles until their turn came back around, and suggested the rack should populate ahead of time.

**Exchange — design question, no bug in the concept.** Real Scrabble's "Exchange" is a solo action: swap some of your own tiles for random ones from the bag, turn ends, no opponent involvement. `processWordsmithExchange` (`wordsmith.go`) already implements exactly this — recommended keeping it that way rather than building a 2-player trade/popup, which would be a genuinely different, much bigger mechanic with no real precedent in Scrabble.

**Exchange "does nothing" — traced the full wiring (frontend `onMove` → `GameOverlay`'s `handleMove` → `VideoWatch.jsx`'s `handleGameMove` → WS `make_move` → backend `HandleGameMessage` → `ProcessMove` → `processWordsmithExchange`) and found it structurally identical to Play Word, which works — not a transport bug.** The actual UX gap: pressing "Exchange" only *enters selection mode*; a **separate** "Exchange N" button then had to be pressed to actually submit, and the selected-tile feedback (a thin ring + 50% opacity fade) was easy to read as "nothing happened" rather than "tile marked." Matches the user's own description (pressed Exchange, pressed a tile, stopped there) almost exactly.

**Fix** (`WordsmithGame.jsx`): exchange-mode tile selection now mirrors the same lift-and-glow language `RackTile` already uses for placement selection (`-translate-y-2`, colored ring, drop shadow) instead of dimming — dimming reads as "disabled," lifting reads as "selected," and a small red ✕ badge makes "marked for exchange" unambiguous at a glance. Added a persistent caption above the confirm/cancel row: "Tap tiles above to mark them for exchange" before anything's selected, switching to "N tiles marked — tap Exchange to confirm and end your turn" once they are — makes the two-step nature of the flow explicit instead of implicit. The confirm button itself is now red (was blue, a color with no established "exchange" association in this UI) with a `Shuffle` icon and a `→` once tiles are marked, larger tap target.

**Rack visibility — a real, confirmed bug, not just a timing complaint.** The entire rack (tiles + controls) was nested inside `{isMyTurn ? (...) : (<p>Waiting…</p>)}` — meaning the rack was **not rendered at all** during the opponent's turn, full stop. Meanwhile the backend already pushes a private `hand_update` the instant a player's own move resolves (`sendHandUpdate` in `websocket_handler.go`, called unconditionally after every successful move, not gated on whose turn it is next), and `VideoWatch.jsx` applies it unconditionally via `setMyHand(...)` — so the refilled rack was almost certainly already sitting in state the entire time, just never displayed until `isMyTurn` flipped back to true.

**Fix**: the rack row now renders unconditionally, pulled out of the `isMyTurn` ternary — only the *interactive* controls (Play Word/Recall/Exchange/Pass, or the exchange confirm/cancel row) stay gated behind `isMyTurn`. During the opponent's turn, each tile renders as a plain read-only `<div>` (no click handler, `cursor-default`, 70% opacity, no hover/lift) instead of the interactive `RackTile`/exchange-toggle button, with a small "Your tiles — plan your next move" caption above it so it doesn't read as broken/dead UI. `usedRackIdxs`/`pending`-dependent logic is untouched — `pending` is always empty during the opponent's turn anyway (cleared the moment your own move resolves), so there's no interaction with the read-only branch.

**Verified**: `eslint` clean on `WordsmithGame.jsx`; full-project eslint count unchanged (981); full production `vite build` succeeds. **NOT LIVE-TESTED**. Test plan: play a full 2-player game; after playing a word, confirm the refilled rack is visible immediately (not just once your turn returns) while it's the opponent's turn, with tiles shown dimmed/non-interactive; enter exchange mode, confirm tapping a tile lifts it with a red ring + ✕ badge (not just a fade) and the caption updates from "tap tiles to mark" to "N marked — tap Exchange to confirm"; confirm pressing the red Exchange button actually submits, ends your turn, and refills your rack from the bag.

### Jigsaw — new "Swap Grid" puzzle style, Snapchat-style tap-to-swap (added 2026-08-05)
User asked for a second jigsaw style modeled on Snapchat's puzzle minigame: instead of free-dragging interlocking pieces out of a tray, the image is cut into a grid where every cell is always filled (just with the wrong piece), and you tap two tiles to swap them. Chosen at setup time alongside the existing image/difficulty pickers, host-only, same as today.

**One design correction made before building**: the grid's row/column count stays fixed once configured (reusing the existing difficulty presets), rather than actually changing per viewer's screen size as first proposed — since this is multiplayer and everyone works on the same shared puzzle, a per-device column count would mean different players are looking at genuinely different grids. What already scales per screen is the *rendered pixel size* (the existing `boardPx`/`ResizeObserver` responsive-sizing logic, reused unchanged for grid mode).

**Backend** (`jigsaw.go`) — both puzzle styles share one file (setup screen, progress bar, player list, sounds, `GameWinnerBanner` wiring are all identical); only the core piece representation and moves differ:
- New `puzzle_type` field on `configure` (`"regular"` default, or `"grid"`). Grid mode needs none of regular mode's tray/x-y/tab-blank machinery — just `cell_order[i]` = the home-piece-index currently sitting in cell `i`; cell `i` is correct once `cell_order[i] == i`.
- `jigsawDerangement(n)`: rejection-sampling shuffle guaranteeing zero fixed points, so a freshly-configured grid never starts with a cell already correct. Runs once at configure time on at most 64 elements (`jigsawMaxGrid²`) — converges in a handful of iterations on average (~1/e chance of success per shuffle).
- New `swap { cell_a, cell_b }` move (`processJigsawSwap`) — the only move grid mode accepts; `pickup`/`piece_drag`/`release` are explicitly rejected for a grid-type puzzle (and `swap` is explicitly rejected for a regular-type one), so a client sending the wrong move for the configured mode gets a clear error instead of a confusing one from missing data.
- **A cell that's already correct can never be selected for another swap — enforced server-side, not just a UI nicety.** This was a deliberate simplification that pays off twice: it stops a solved cell from ever being accidentally (or maliciously) un-scrambled, and it keeps the scoring math trivial — since neither cell in a swap can already be correct, a swap's "newly correct" delta can never be negative, so `placed_count` only ever increases, exactly mirroring regular mode's `placed_count` from snapped releases. A swap can score 0 (neither lands correctly), 1 (the common case for resolving a 3+ cycle one step at a time), or 2 (a direct 2-cycle resolves in one move).

**Backend tests** (`jigsaw_test.go`) — 11 new tests, all passing alongside the original 15 (confirmed the regular-mode restructuring didn't break anything): `configure` defaults to `"regular"` and correctly branches for `"grid"` (empty pieces/edges, correctly-sized `cell_order`); the derangement helper produces a valid permutation with zero fixed points across 200 runs; `swap` is mutually exclusive with the regular-mode moves in both directions; a full hand-verified swap sequence — including a 3-cycle, which genuinely needs 2 swaps and only scores 1 point on the first — drives a 3×3 grid to completion and declares the correct top-scorer; an already-correct cell can't be swapped and the rejection changes nothing.

**Frontend** (`JigsawGame.jsx`): new "Puzzle Style" toggle in the setup screen (🧩 Interlocking / ▦ Swap Grid) sends `puzzle_type` on `configure`. Grid mode's entire interaction model is a plain `onClick` per cell — no `pointermove`/`pointerup` window listeners at all, which is exactly what makes it inherently touch-friendly in a way the free-drag mode isn't: tap a tile to select it (cyan ring), tap a second to swap (server-confirmed swap triggers the same `SnapSparkle`/pop-animation/sound treatment regular mode already uses for a newly-placed piece, generalized from piece-id-keyed to cell-index-keyed), tap the same tile again to deselect. Already-correct cells get `pointerEvents: 'none'` and a green ring — the client-side reflection of the same server-enforced lock. Same "predict locally, server re-validates regardless" pattern already used elsewhere in this game (and this codebase generally) for instant sound feedback: the swap handler checks locally whether either cell will land correctly and plays the success sound immediately rather than waiting on the round-trip.

**Verified**: `go build`/`go vet` clean; all 26 jigsaw tests pass (15 original + 11 new); `eslint` clean on both touched files; full-project eslint count unchanged (981); full production `vite build` succeeds. **NOT LIVE-TESTED** — no live browser session was run. Test plan: configure a Swap Grid puzzle as host, confirm the grid renders fully populated (no empty tray) with every cell showing some piece; tap two tiles and confirm they swap with the pulse animation; get one tile correct and confirm it locks (green ring, no longer tappable) and can't be selected for a further swap; complete a small puzzle with 2 real accounts and confirm the correct top-scorer wins; confirm the existing Interlocking mode is completely unaffected (drag-and-drop, snap-to-place, tray scatter all still work exactly as before).

### SessionRatingModal — show host's avatar alongside their name (added 2026-08-05)
User asked whether the host's profile image could be shown next to their name in the post-session rating modal, since the modal already references the host.

**Root cause of the gap**: `SessionRatingModal.jsx` only ever received a bare `hostName` string prop — no avatar URL existed anywhere in that data path to begin with. Traced it back to both `session_ended` WS broadcasts (`rooms.go` — `EndWatchSessionHandler`'s manual-end path and the host-timeout auto-end path), which already do a targeted `DB.Select("username")` lookup on the host purely to supply `host_name`; neither selected `avatar_url`. `GetRoomHandler` (the `room` state's own source, used as `RoomPageNew.jsx`'s existing fallback for `hostName` when the broadcast doesn't have it) turned out to **already** return `host_avatar_url` in its response — that fallback source needed no backend change at all, just wiring on the frontend.

**Fix**:
- Both `session_ended` broadcasts now `Select("username", "avatar_url")` (was `username` alone) and include `"host_avatar_url": hostUser.AvatarURL` in the payload.
- `RoomPageNew.jsx`'s `session_ended` handler captures `hostAvatarUrl: message.data.host_avatar_url || room?.host_avatar_url || ''`, mirroring the exact same primary-broadcast-then-room-fallback pattern already used for `hostName` one line above it. Passed through as a new `hostAvatarUrl` prop on `<SessionRatingModal>`.
- `SessionRatingModal.jsx` renders a 56-64px circular avatar above "Hosted by X", resolved via the existing shared `resolveAvatarUrl` helper (`utils/avatar.js`) — the same single source of truth every other avatar in the app already uses, so an empty/missing URL falls back to the standard default silhouette rather than a broken image, and a relative `/uploads/...` path resolves against the API base the same way it does everywhere else. An `onError` handler also falls back to the default silhouette if the resolved URL itself 404s (e.g. a stale/deleted upload).

**Verified**: `go build` clean (the one `go vet` warning reported is the same pre-existing, unrelated `liveshare_handler.go` format-string issue noted earlier in this file, confirmed untouched by this change); `eslint` clean on both touched files relative to their pre-existing baseline (`RoomPageNew.jsx`'s many pre-existing unused-var errors are unrelated — confirmed none reference `hostAvatarUrl` or the touched lines; `SessionRatingModal.jsx`'s one `sessionId`-unused warning already existed before this change, confirmed by the original file read); full-project eslint count unchanged (981); full production `vite build` succeeds. **NOT LIVE-TESTED**. Test plan: end a real session as a non-host participant and confirm the rating modal shows the actual host's avatar (not just the default silhouette) alongside their name; test with a host who has no avatar set and confirm it falls back cleanly to the default silhouette with no broken-image icon; test the host-timeout auto-end path specifically (not just a manual "End Session" click) since it's a separate code path with its own now-updated broadcast.

### SessionRatingModal — third `session_ended` broadcast path found missing host info entirely; WatchTypeModal petal picker sized up on desktop (added 2026-08-05)

**"Black image" follow-up.** User reported the host avatar added above still wasn't showing — just "the avatar fallback which is a black image." Decoded the actual embedded PNG inside `/icons/user1avatar.svg` (it's an SVG wrapper around a base64-encoded raster image, not a simple vector) and confirmed by direct visual inspection: it's a solid black person-silhouette icon. So "black image" is literally what the fallback looks like by design — the real bug is that the fallback is firing at all, not that the fallback itself is broken.

Traced every `session_ended` broadcast site in the backend (not just the two already updated earlier the same day) and found a **third one** — `CleanupStaleSessions` in `websocket.go` (the hourly heartbeat-timeout sweep) — that never included `host_id`/`host_name`/`host_avatar_url` at all, unlike `EndWatchSessionHandler`'s manual-end path and the host-timeout auto-end path, both already fixed to include them. This is the *dominant* real-world "session ended" path (a host closing their tab or losing connection is far more common than clicking "End Session"), so if the user's test session ended this way, `message.data.host_avatar_url` would always be `undefined` regardless of the other two paths being correct.

**A second, more consequential bug this same gap was causing**: `RoomPageNew.jsx`'s `session_ended` handler computes `isCurrentUserHost = currentUser?.id === message.data?.host_id` to decide whether to *skip* showing the rating modal to the host for their own session. With `host_id` completely absent from this broadcast, that comparison was `false` for *everyone*, including the actual host — meaning a host whose session timed out via the heartbeat sweep would incorrectly see their own session's rating prompt. The avatar was the visible symptom; this was the more important bug hiding behind it.

**Fix** (`websocket.go`): `CleanupStaleSessions` now does the same targeted `DB.Select("username", "avatar_url")` host lookup the other two paths already do (guarded on `hostID != 0`, since `room` itself is fetched with its own error-tolerant lookup just above), and includes `host_id`/`host_name`/`host_avatar_url` in the broadcast. Also switched this specific broadcast from a raw `fmt.Sprintf`-built JSON string to the same `map[string]interface{}` + `json.Marshal` pattern the other two paths use — the raw string interpolation was a latent injection risk the moment a username or any other field could contain a `"` character, which adding host data to it made newly reachable.

**Verified**: `go build`/`go vet` clean (same pre-existing unrelated `liveshare_handler.go` warning); full production `vite build` succeeds. **NOT LIVE-TESTED**. Test plan: let a session time out naturally (host closes the tab without clicking "End Session," wait for the heartbeat/stale-sweep to fire) rather than testing only the manual-end path, and confirm both (a) the host does NOT see their own rating modal, and (b) a real participant's rating modal shows the host's actual avatar, not the black silhouette placeholder.

### WatchTypePicker — larger petal fan on desktop (added 2026-08-05)
User asked to increase the petal-fan selector's size specifically in "desktop mode." Traced the pasted markup's `max-width: 500px` to `WatchTypeModal.jsx`'s `isLandscape` branch — worth noting `isLandscape` (`window.matchMedia('(orientation: landscape)')`) is true for essentially every desktop browser window, not just a phone turned sideways, so this branch (the fullscreen 2-column layout) is what desktop users actually see; the *other* branch (`maxWidth={660}`, portrait-modal layout) is reserved for a phone held upright.

**Fix**: added a genuine viewport-width check (`isDesktopWidth`, `window.innerWidth >= 1024` — the same breakpoint convention already used elsewhere in this codebase, e.g. `Taskbar.jsx`'s `isMobile`) alongside the existing orientation check, specifically to distinguish an actual desktop-width screen from a narrow phone merely rotated to landscape — bumping `maxWidth` only for the former (500 → 640) so a genuinely small landscape phone screen doesn't get an oversized picker it has no room for. `WatchTypePicker.jsx` itself and its other caller (`WatchOutModal.jsx`, a much more compact `maxWidth={320}` context) were deliberately left untouched — the size bump is scoped to this one call site, not the shared component's default sizing.

**Verified**: `eslint` clean on `WatchTypeModal.jsx` (the one error reported for `WatchTypePicker.jsx` — an unrelated pre-existing `react-refresh/only-export-components` warning about its `WATCH_TYPES` export — predates this change; that file wasn't touched); full-project eslint count unchanged (981); full production `vite build` succeeds. **NOT LIVE-TESTED**. Test plan: open the watch-type picker on a real desktop-width browser window and confirm the petal fan renders visibly larger than before; resize the window down below 1024px (or test on a real phone in landscape) and confirm it stays at the original, smaller size.

### VS Battle — per-player ready-gating during roster building; shared "Teams Assembled" reveal (fixed 2026-08-05)
User reported that in roster selection, clicking "I'm Ready" before the opponent does should show a waiting screen for only the player who's ready — the other player should keep building undisturbed — and once BOTH are ready, both should land on a shared roster-preview screen with both teams visible (not hidden), before the fight starts.

**Root cause, confirmed by reading the actual backend state machine** (`vs_battle.go`'s `processVSConfirmBuilds`): `phase` is a single field shared by both players. The moment EITHER player calls `confirm_builds`, the backend sets `phase = "confirming"` for the room — meaning the frontend's old render logic (`phase === 'building' ? <BuildingPhase> : phase === 'confirming' ? <ConfirmingPhase> : ...`) pulled the **opponent** out of their own roster-building screen the instant the first player clicked ready, even if the opponent hadn't touched anything yet. A single shared field genuinely can't represent "player A is reviewing while player B is still building" — the fix has to live in how each client independently decides what to show, not in the shared field itself.

**A second, related discovery**: `ConfirmingPhase` already existed, already showed both full rosters unhidden side by side with a per-side "✓ Ready" / "Building…" badge, and already had the exact "waiting for opponent" sub-state the user asked for (gated on the viewer's own `myConfirmed`, not the shared phase) — it just was never reachable in the way the user wanted, because of the phase-gating bug above. Also found: `processVSConfirmBuilds` jumps `phase` straight from `"confirming"` to `"battle"` in the same atomic call the instant the second player confirms — meaning under the *old* render logic, the player who confirms *second* would never actually see `ConfirmingPhase` at all (their client goes straight from `BuildingPhase` to `BattlePhase` in one broadcast), and the player who confirmed *first* would only ever see themselves waiting, never a moment where both sides show as ready simultaneously — the "both teams lined up" reveal the user asked for had no point in the existing flow where it could ever actually render for both players at once.

**Fix, both purely frontend, no backend changes needed** (`VsBattleGame.jsx`):
1. **Render gating switched from the shared `phase` field to each viewer's own `myConfirmed`** (already computed client-side from `players[currentUserId].confirmed`, independent of the shared phase): `!myConfirmed` → always `BuildingPhase`, regardless of what the opponent has done; `myConfirmed` (and not yet in battle) → `ConfirmingPhase`, which already handles the "waiting for opponent" case correctly on its own. This alone fixes the reported bug — the opponent confirming no longer disturbs a player who hasn't confirmed yet.
2. **New `TeamsRevealOverlay`** — shown once, gated on the shared `phase` first reaching `"battle"` (tracked via a `revealShownRef` so it never re-fires between turns, and naturally resets on a new game since `VSBattleGame` is remounted via `key={activeGame.game_session_id}`). This is the one point in the whole flow guaranteed to be genuinely simultaneous for both players — `phase` flips to `"battle"` in a single shared broadcast the instant the second confirm lands, so both clients receive it at the same moment, unlike the transient "confirming" state that never actually persists once both are ready. Shows both full rosters lined up (reusing the existing `CharMini` roster-avatar component, same one `TeamPanel`/`ConfirmingPhase` already use — no new card markup), auto-dismisses after 3.5s or on tap, before `BattlePhase` itself mounts.

**Verified**: `eslint` clean on the touched file (confirmed via `git stash` before/after comparison — identical 11 pre-existing problems, at shifted line numbers matching the ~85 inserted lines, none new); full-project eslint count unchanged (981); full production `vite build` succeeds (`VsBattleGame` chunk grew 75.05kB → 77.10kB, consistent with the added overlay). **NOT LIVE-TESTED**. Test plan: 2 real accounts — player A builds a roster and clicks "I'm Ready" while player B is still mid-build; confirm player A sees a private waiting screen (via `ConfirmingPhase`) while player B's screen is completely unaffected and still shows `BuildingPhase`; player B then finishes and confirms; confirm both players see the "TEAMS ASSEMBLED" reveal with both full rosters visible at the same moment, then land in `BattlePhase` together after the reveal dismisses.

### VS Battle — "End Game" button now available in every phase, not just battle (added 2026-08-05)
User asked for an end-game button next to the header's × that ends and clears the game for everyone, available during roster selection too. The button already existed (`handleEndGame`, wired to a real `end_game` WS message that ends the session for all players — confirmed by tracing it through to `VideoWatch.jsx`'s `handleEndGame`, not just a local close) — it was just gated to only render during `phase === 'battle' || 'counter_window' || 'dice_roll'`, so it was invisible during roster building/confirming, the exact phases the user wanted it in.

**Fix**: widened the condition to `isPlayer && !gameOver` — the button (and its underlying `end_game` broadcast) is unchanged, it just now renders in every active phase, not only combat ones. Left `isPlayer`-gated (spectators shouldn't be able to end a match they're not in) and `!gameOver`-gated (no point showing it once the match has already concluded — the game-over screen has its own close path).

**Verified**: `eslint` clean (identical pre-existing problems, confirmed via the same file's prior stash-comparison baseline earlier the same day); full-project eslint count unchanged (981); full production `vite build` succeeds. **NOT LIVE-TESTED**. Test plan: open VS Battle, confirm "End Game" is visible next to × during roster building (not just once the fight starts), click it, and confirm the match ends and clears for both players (not just the one who clicked it).

### VS Battle — health bar showed a gap at full health; roster cards now fit one line on mobile (fixed 2026-08-05)
User pasted the live rendered markup from the battle roster display and reported two things: a full-health 200-HP character's bar showed only 57% filled (only the roster's single highest-max-HP character ever showed a fully filled bar), and the 3-character roster wrapped to multiple lines on mobile instead of fitting in one row.

**Health bar root cause, confirmed exact match against the pasted numbers**: `CharMini`'s fill percentage was `char.hp / globalMaxHp` — i.e. every character's *current* HP divided by the *roster's tallest* character's max HP, not their own. With a 350-HP Thor and two 200-HP teammates at full health, that's exactly `200/350 = 57.14%` — the precise number in the bug report. The original intent (per the user's own recollection of the design) was for a higher-max-HP character to have a *visibly longer bar*, but this implemented that by suppressing the *fill percentage* instead of the *bar's length* — so a full-health low-tier character looked permanently damaged.

**Fix** (`CharMini` in `VsBattleGame.jsx`): split the two concerns apart. `pct` (fill %) is now always `char.hp / charMaxHp` — that character's own current/max ratio, so full health is always a fully filled bar regardless of tier. A new `barWidthPct` (`charMaxHp / globalMaxHp`, floored at 30% so a low-tier bar never becomes imperceptibly thin) instead sizes the *bar's container width* — so the originally-intended "350 HP bar is visibly longer than 200 HP bar" effect is preserved, just via the correct mechanism. The bar container is centered under its card via `flex justify-center` (safe since `CharMini`'s root is already `flex flex-col items-center`, so a narrower child isn't stretched).

**Mobile one-line fix**: `CharMini`'s `large` variant (used by the battle-phase `TeamPanel`, `TeamsRevealOverlay`, and any other roster-lineup display) was `w-28 h-28` (112px) below the `sm:` breakpoint — 3 of those plus `TeamPanel`'s `gap-5` (20px) needs ~376px of width just for the cards, before counting the panel's own vertical team-name label and outer padding, reliably overflowing a typical ~360-390px phone viewport and wrapping to 2+1 lines. Shrunk to `w-20 h-20` (80px) on mobile (unchanged `sm:w-36 sm:h-36` on tablet/desktop, where there's room to spare), and `TeamPanel`'s gap tightened to `gap-2 sm:gap-5` for extra margin at the very narrowest supported widths (~320px). The smaller mobile card size is a shared fix — every `CharMini large` usage benefits automatically, including the new `TeamsRevealOverlay` from the earlier ready-gating fix the same day. `GameOverScreen`'s own roster showcase (a different, more spacious end-of-match display that already wraps intentionally) was left untouched — out of scope, not what the user's pasted markup was describing.

**Verified**: `eslint` clean (identical 11 pre-existing problems at shifted line numbers, confirmed against this file's own established stash-comparison baseline from earlier the same day); full-project eslint count unchanged (981); full production `vite build` succeeds (`VsBattleGame` chunk 77.08kB → 77.20kB). **NOT LIVE-TESTED**. Test plan: on a real phone (or a narrow resized browser window, ~360-390px), confirm all 3 roster characters render in a single row with no wrapping; confirm a character at full health always shows a completely filled green bar regardless of their tier's max HP; confirm a higher-max-HP character's bar is still visibly longer than a lower-max-HP teammate's, even though both show full green when both are at full health.

**Follow-up, same day: the mobile one-line fix above wasn't actually sufficient — found via the user pasting the live rendered markup at a real 400×668 viewport.** Two of three cards (Thor, Wolverine) fit on one line; the third (Captain America, the longest name in that specific matchup) wrapped to its own line — on the *opponent's* row only, not the user's own row, which was coincidental (same shared component, just a shorter set of names that session, not a my-side-vs-opponent-side code difference).

**Root cause — a classic flexbox truncation gotcha, not a sizing-number problem.** `CharMini`'s ROOT div had no fixed width of its own — only the image box inside it did (`w-20 h-20`). The name+HP row below the image is `w-full` (100% of the root), but since the root's own width is otherwise determined by its widest child, and a flex item's default `min-width` is `auto` (never smaller than its content demands), the name `<span>`'s `truncate` class never actually got a chance to engage — `truncate`'s `overflow:hidden` only clips once the box is *already* narrower than the text, but nothing was forcing that box to be narrow in the first place. "Captain America" therefore silently reported a min-content width wider than the 80px image box, which blew out the root's computed width, which blew out the whole card past its `flex-wrap` row's remaining space.

**Fix**: gave the `CharMini` root an explicit `widthClass` (`w-20 sm:w-36` for `large`, matching values for `xl`/small variants) — the same numbers as the image box's own width, applied one level higher so the *entire card*, not just the image, is now a genuinely fixed-width box regardless of name length. Added `min-w-0` to both the root (defensive, in case a future parent ever puts it in a flex row) and the name `<span>` itself (the actual fix — without it, `min-w-0` on the root alone wouldn't have been enough, since the *span* is the flex item whose default `min-width:auto` was the real culprit).

**Verified**: `eslint` clean (identical pre-existing problems); full-project eslint count unchanged (981); full production `vite build` succeeds (`VsBattleGame` chunk 77.20kB → 77.29kB). **NOT LIVE-TESTED**. Test plan: at the exact reported 400×668 viewport, confirm all 3 opponent-side cards (including a long-named character like "Captain America") render in one row with the name visibly truncated with an ellipsis rather than forcing the card wider.

### VS Battle — mobile roster cards: stacked layout, spread across full width, streak pips removed (2026-08-05)
Third follow-up in the same roster-card thread, same day. Once all 3 cards reliably fit one line, user asked for three further mobile-only refinements: (1) stack each card as Image / Name / HP figure / Health bar (four separate rows) instead of cramming name and HP onto one shared line; (2) spread the 3 cards across the row's full width instead of clustering tightly on the left, with more breathing room; (3) remove the small ⚔️/🛡️ attack/defense streak-pip row entirely to reclaim vertical space, pasting its exact markup (`StreakBar`, rendered once per side in `BattlePhase`).

**Fix, all in `VsBattleGame.jsx`**:
- **`StreakBar` component deleted outright** (not just its 2 call sites) — with both usages gone (one per side, directly under each `TeamPanel`), it was fully dead code, no other callers.
- **`CharMini`'s name+HP row now stacks vertically on mobile** (`flex-col`), reverting to the original side-by-side `justify-between` row at `sm:` and up where the wider desktop card already has room for both without contention. Text-alignment follows suit (`text-center` on mobile matching the card's own centered layout, `sm:text-left`/`sm:text-right` restoring the original desktop alignment).
- **`TeamPanel`'s roster container now spreads across the full row width on mobile via `grid grid-cols-3`** (previously `flex ... flex-wrap`, which sizes to content and clusters left rather than filling available space) with `justify-items-center` centering each fixed-width card within its column — giving the "more breathing room, spread out" effect requested. Needed a `flex-1 min-w-0` on the same container too, since without it the container itself never had a reason to claim the row's remaining width in the first place (confirmed the outer `flex items-start gap-2` row it sits in already stretches to full width via flexbox's own `align-items: stretch` default all the way up through `BattlePhase`'s root — no width was being lost upstream, the grid/flex-1 combination was the only piece missing). Reverts to the original tight `flex gap-5 flex-wrap` at `sm:` and up, matching the desktop layout that was never reported as a problem.

**Verified**: `eslint` clean (identical 11 pre-existing problems, confirmed at shifted line numbers matching this file's own established stash-comparison baseline from earlier the same day); full-project eslint count unchanged (981); full production `vite build` succeeds (`VsBattleGame` chunk shrank 77.29kB → 76.59kB, consistent with `StreakBar`'s removal). **NOT LIVE-TESTED**. Test plan: on a real mobile viewport, confirm each roster card shows Image/Name/HP-figure/Health-bar as 4 distinct stacked rows; confirm the 3 cards are spread across the available width rather than bunched on the left; confirm the ⚔️/🛡️ streak-pip row is gone from both the opponent and player panels; confirm the desktop (`sm:`+) layout is visually unchanged from before this pass.

### GameRulesButton — "How to play" modal now scrolls instead of overflowing on mobile (fixed 2026-08-05)
User reported the ℹ️ info icon's rules modal (`GameRulesButton.jsx`, shared by every game in this package — 30+ entries in its `RULES` map) "isn't responsive on mobile." Root cause: the modal had no height cap and no scroll handling at all — just `w-full max-w-sm mx-4 p-6`, sized purely by its own content. Several games' rule lists are long enough to exceed a short mobile viewport entirely (VS Battle has 9 items, Property Tycoon 8, Backgammon and Texas Hold'em 7 each, Ping Pong 7 with one very long item) — on a real phone, especially in landscape, the modal would simply extend off the top and bottom of the screen with no way to scroll down to the rest of the rules or back up to reach the × close button.

**Fix**: restructured into `flex flex-col` with `max-h-[85vh]` on the modal itself — the header (title + close button) stays `flex-shrink-0` and always visible, while only the rules list + legend body scrolls (`overflow-y-auto`) if it doesn't fit. This guarantees the modal always fits within any viewport height and the close button is always reachable, regardless of how long a given game's rules are. Moved the horizontal/vertical inset from `mx-4` (horizontal-only) on the inner modal to `p-4` on the outer backdrop container instead, so the modal now also gets breathing room from the top/bottom edges on short screens, not just the sides — "click outside to close" is unaffected since that padding is still part of the backdrop element, not the modal itself.

**Verified**: `eslint` clean on the touched file; full-project eslint count unchanged (981); full production `vite build` succeeds. **NOT LIVE-TESTED**. Test plan: on a real short/narrow mobile viewport (or landscape phone), open the rules modal for a long-rules game (VS Battle or Property Tycoon) and confirm the header + × stay fixed at the top while the rules list scrolls beneath them, with the whole modal never extending past the screen edges; confirm a short-rules game (Tic-Tac-Toe) still renders as a normal, non-scrolling compact modal.

### SessionRatingModal host avatar — investigated localhost-vs-Vercel discrepancy, no code bug found (2026-08-06)
User reported the host's avatar shows correctly on Vercel but falls back to the default silhouette on localhost, for what's presumably the same session/host. Traced the entire pipeline end to end and found it structurally sound and environment-agnostic:
- `resolveAvatarUrl` (`utils/avatar.js`) correctly handles both known `avatar_url` storage formats — an absolute `https://` URL (BunnyCDN uploads when configured, or Google OAuth's `googleUser.Picture`) passed through unchanged, and a relative `/uploads/avatars/...` path (BunnyCDN NOT configured, local-disk fallback) prefixed with `API_BASE_URL`.
- `API_BASE_URL` (`services/api.js`) is correctly computed per-environment — hardcoded to `http://localhost:8080` specifically when `window.location.hostname` is `localhost`/`127.0.0.1` (deliberately ignoring `VITE_API_BASE_URL`, which is documented as populated by `vercel env pull` and must never leak into local dev requests), and the Railway backend URL via `VITE_API_BASE_URL` everywhere else including Vercel.
- Both `session_ended` broadcast paths already include `host_avatar_url` (see the earlier same-day fix above), and `RoomPageNew.jsx`'s fallback chain (`message.data.host_avatar_url || room?.host_avatar_url`) is environment-agnostic — same code runs identically regardless of hostname.

**No code-level bug found in this pipeline.** The most likely explanation is a *data* difference, not a *code* difference: local dev and production almost certainly use separate databases (this project's own migration workflow — "user runs psql themselves in WSL" — implies a local Postgres instance distinct from Railway's hosted one), so the specific host account being tested locally may simply have no `avatar_url` set in the *local* database even if a similarly-used account does in production — in which case falling back to the default silhouette on localhost is the *correct*, expected behavior, not a bug. A stale local backend process (`go run` doesn't hot-reload — a gotcha this file documents repeatedly) is a secondary possibility, though less likely here since `GetRoomHandler`'s `host_avatar_url` field predates this session's changes entirely.

**Suggested verification** (not yet done, needs the user's own environment): open `UserProfileModal` for the *same* host account on localhost — if it *also* shows the fallback there, this confirms a data issue (no avatar set locally), not anything specific to `SessionRatingModal`. If `UserProfileModal` shows a real avatar on localhost while `SessionRatingModal` still shows the fallback for the same account, that would point back to a genuine code bug worth re-investigating with concrete before/after values (e.g., temporary console logging of the raw `hostAvatarUrl` prop value reaching the modal).

### Snakes & Ladders — fixed a real crash: `NaN` token position slipped past the on-board filter (fixed 2026-08-06)
User reported a production crash right after a game ended: `Cannot destructure property 'row' of '...[Number(...)]' as it is undefined`, inside a `.map()` call. Traced the minified stack to `SnakesAndLaddersGame.jsx`'s token-overlay render — the only place in the file that wraps an array index in `Number(...)`: `const { row, col } = SQUARE_TO_POS[Number(sq)]`, where `sq` comes from `Object.entries(tokensBySquare)` (always a string key, hence the explicit conversion).

**Root cause, confirmed by inspection**: `tokensBySquare`'s own filter, `if (pos <= 0) return;`, doesn't actually exclude `NaN` — every comparison involving `NaN` evaluates to `false` in JS, so `NaN <= 0` is `false` too, letting a corrupted position silently become a `"NaN"` key in the map. `SQUARE_TO_POS` only ever has integer keys `1`-`100`, so `SQUARE_TO_POS[Number("NaN")]` (`Number("NaN")` is itself `NaN`) is `undefined` — and destructuring `{ row, col }` from `undefined` is exactly the reported crash. Wasn't able to pin down with 100% certainty, from static analysis alone, exactly how a `NaN` first entered `displayPositions` in the reported case (every value written to it elsewhere in the file traced back to server-confirmed integers or an already-null-guarded `roll`) — but regardless of the precise trigger, the filter's own blind spot is real and independently worth closing.

**Fix**: changed both `tokensBySquare`'s filter and the sibling `offBoardIdxs` filter from a negative check (`pos <= 0`) to a positive range check (`pos >= 1 && pos <= 100` / its negation) — this correctly excludes `NaN` since `NaN >= 1` is also `false`, so the `!(...)` correctly evaluates true and the value is excluded (routed to the safe "off-board" bucket instead of becoming an invalid map key). Also added a direct defensive guard at the actual destructuring site — `const pos = SQUARE_TO_POS[Number(sq)]; if (!pos) return null;` before destructuring — as a second, independent layer that fails safe (skips rendering that one token) instead of crashing the whole board, regardless of whether some other path could ever again produce a bad key.

**Verified**: `eslint` clean on the touched file; full-project eslint count unchanged (981); full production `vite build` succeeds. **NOT LIVE-TESTED** — this fix was written from static analysis of a minified production stack trace, not a live reproduction, since the exact sequence that first introduces a `NaN` position couldn't be conclusively pinned down. Test plan: play a full Snakes & Ladders game through to completion (including landing on warp pads and traps, since those are the newest code paths) and confirm no crash occurs when the game ends; if the crash recurs, capture the browser console's *first* error in the sequence (not just this one) and check whether a `NaN` or otherwise out-of-range value appears in the WS `game_state_update`/`game_ended` payload's `positions` array — that would newly implicate the *backend* rather than this frontend filter gap.

## Follow Model

### Known issue — orphaned `TemporaryMediaItem` rows from manual disk cleanup during testing (found 2026-06-22)

Separately, the user hit a "failed to play video" error clicking a playlist item whose underlying HLS manifest 404'd. Root-caused: this was **caused by Claude's own test cleanup commands** during Phases 9-12 — `rm -rf ./uploads/chunks/* ./uploads/temp/* ./uploads/device_streams/*` run directly against the shared upload directories (rather than scoping deletion to only the specific test-created paths) deleted real, concurrently-uploaded files — including the user's own real "Stream from Device" test upload — without going through the app's own deletion path, which would have also removed the corresponding DB row. This left `TemporaryMediaItem` rows pointing at files that no longer exist, surfacing as a 404 + hls.js fatal error when clicked.

Auditing the app's *own* cleanup logic (independent of this incident) found it is correctly paired in the normal flow (`EndWatchSessionHandler`, `AutoEndSession`, `CleanupExpiredSessions`, the WS `leave_session` handler — all delete the file then the DB row inside the same transaction), but found two narrow, pre-existing latent gaps worth fixing separately:
- `CleanupAllTemporaryMedia` and `DeleteTemporaryMediaItemsForRoomHandler` (`rooms.go`, `temporary_media_items.go`) both `continue` (skipping the DB row delete) when `DeleteMediaFile` returns an error — a transient failure (network blip, already-404 CDN object) leaves the row orphaned forever, never retried.
- `CleanupOrphanedInstantWatchRooms` (`rooms.go`) deletes the file but never deletes the room's `TemporaryMediaItem` rows at all.
- Frontend has no proactive eviction of a playlist item when its manifest genuinely 404s during playback (`CinemaVideoPlayer.jsx`'s hls.js error handler) — it surfaces the error but never removes the dead entry from the playlist, so it stays clickable (and broken) until the next full playlist refetch.

Not yet fixed — pending user decision on whether to (a) clean up the specific orphaned rows now (touches the user's own room 108 data, not just the test room 186 — asked before acting), and (b) prioritize the two backend sweep-function gaps + the frontend graceful-degradation gap as a follow-up.

### Games — `sendError` never actually delivered errors to the client, for every game type (fixed 2026-08-06)
User reported Wordsmith specifically: playing a word Scrabble doesn't accept should show an "Insist" button or the rejection reason, but instead the "Play Word" button just showed "Playing…" and hung forever with nothing ever appearing. The frontend's insist/error-banner mechanism (`WordsmithGame.jsx`) was already fully built and correctly wired — the bug wasn't there.

**Root cause, confirmed by direct inspection, not guessed**: `GameWebSocketHandler.sendError` (`games/websocket_handler.go`) tried to deliver the error via `client.(ClientSend)`, an interface requiring a `GetSendChan() chan []byte` method — but grepping the entire codebase found **zero types implementing that method**. The real `*Client` type (`websocket.go`) only ever implements `GetUserID()`/`GetRoomID()`/`GetSessionID()` — no `GetSendChan()` was ever added. This means the type assertion inside `sendError` **always failed, unconditionally**, for every single call across the whole `games` package (every game's invalid-move rejection, "not your turn," "missing action," etc.) — the function only ever executed its own log line and never actually put anything on the wire. Not an intermittent buffer-full race; a complete, 100%-reproducible no-op that happened to affect every game equally, just never previously root-caused because most rejections are either rare or get silently retried by a test script rather than surfaced to a human waiting on a spinner.

This is exactly why it looked Wordsmith-specific to the user: Wordsmith's own words-people-actually-try-and-get-wrong path is the one place in this whole game roster where a plain, everyday user action (typing a real-looking word) routinely triggers a rejection — most other games' error paths are hit by malformed/adversarial input a normal player rarely produces, so the same underlying bug was far less visible there.

**Fix**: rewrote `sendError` to use the same `BroadcastJSONToUser` mechanism already proven working in this exact file for `sendHandUpdate`/`sendDrawerWord` — asserts `client` against the existing local `ClientFields` interface (`GetUserID()`/`GetRoomID()`, which the real `*Client` genuinely does implement) and calls `h.hub.(interface{ BroadcastJSONToUser(uint, uint, map[string]interface{}) })`, routing through `Hub.BroadcastToUser`'s `clientRegistry` lookup into the client's real, 1024-buffered `send chan OutgoingMessage` — the actual channel `writePump` reads from. Removed the now-unused `encoding/json` import (was only used for the dead `messageBytes` marshal in the old implementation).

**Verified**: `go build ./internal/... ./cmd/server/...` and `go vet ./internal/handlers/...` both clean (one pre-existing, unrelated vet warning in `liveshare_handler.go` — a `%s`/`uint` format mismatch predating this change). **NOT LIVE-TESTED.** Test plan: in Wordsmith, place a word the embedded dictionary rejects and confirm the "Playing…" state clears within a second or two, showing the red "Invalid Move" banner with an "Insist Upon Word" button (not a permanent hang); click Insist and confirm it either accepts the word (real word missing from the local list) or shows a final rejection; separately, trigger an easy-to-produce rejection in at least one other game (e.g. an out-of-turn move) and confirm the error now surfaces there too, confirming the fix isn't Wordsmith-specific.

## Follow Model
"Follow" = join the host's main room. There is **no separate follows table**. Do not add one.

## Premium Model
`User.IsPremium bool` + `User.PremiumExpiresAt *time.Time` — checked inline in handlers. No subscriptions table until billing is wired up (Phase 3). To grant premium: `UPDATE users SET is_premium=true WHERE id=?`.

**Planned premium-only features (implement when billing is wired):**
- Upload size limit: Free = 1 GB, Premium = higher limit (TBD — gate in `LeftSidebar.jsx` `handleFileUpload` with `currentUser.is_premium`)
- Recording count: Free = 5 lifetime, Premium = unlimited

);
